## Alloyed Construct
' is a Banner Unit of the Umbra . It revolves around a unique fuel mechanic where it can't attack until it has been fed at least one morsel.

    1.  Lore
As their understanding of the world around them grew, the Umbra were able to create autonomous machines to do the work of one thousand Morsels. They've since served the additional roles of Crucible defender and my personal guard into the depths of Hell.

    1.  Strategy
Since this creature can't attack on the first turn, it is most effectively used on the 2nd or 3rd floor so that it has time to be fueled before it needs to fight. Given that this minion has multi-strike, Morsels that grant extra attack, such as Magma Morsels are the most effective Morsels to feed this with.

Spells such as Furnace Tap can be used to great effect, due to the unit's already destructive attack power.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
| Quick is very good on  as unlike other Gorge Units,  does not have innate sustain and quick allows it to kill enemy units and avoid taking too much damage before relentless.
|-
|
| Strengthstone is effective on units with Multistrike (especially units with a low default Attack value). The additional Attack Attack bonus is effectively applied multiple times on units with Multistrike.
|-
|
| Frenzystone is effective on units with high attack, moreso with units that already have Multistrike.
|}

    1.  Pacts & Unit Essence (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)
The drawback of Alloyed Construct's essence, namely the Inert status, has no additional effect on Alloyed Construct itself. Hence, it is a good idea to get a second copy of Alloyed Construct and merge the two: not only do you get one more Multistrike on a powerful unit, the Gorge ability is also duplicated, yielding 2 Fuel for each morsel gorged.

    1.  History
See: Version History

!Version

## Alpha Fiend
' is a Banner Unit of the Hellhorned

    1.  Lore
These ancient demons of the Hellhorned live a reclusive life on the edge of the Scorched Plains. Their isolated existence has made them even more difficult to communicate with, but they seem to understand the peril their clan is in since they've joined me on this perilous journey.

    1.  Strategy

' really wants Multistrike. Grrgh's Goad, Onehorn's Tome, or the Strike Hard mutator are extremely partners for '.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Frenzystone is a very important upgrade on ' because its Strike ability is triggered for each attack, scaling its damage twice as fast and utilizing that damage to double the effect. Provided you can protect ', it can very much be worthwhile to give it 2 Frenzystones.
|-
|
|Heartstone can be a good upgrade on ' thanks to its respectable base Health and the difficulty of putting a bulkier unit in front of it due to its Capacity. For it to be gaining strength, it needs to be fighting enemies, which means it will be taking damage if it is on the front line.
|}

    1.  Pacts & Unit Essence
Although its decent stats are not reflected by its unit essence, its most notable feature, the attack gain on strike, retains its full potency in unit essence form. Given this, the fact multistrike is almost always the best way to capitalize on this ability, and that all multistrike essences come with some kind of negative consequence, it's generally best to sacrifice Alpha Fiend into another unit rather than the other way around, provided you can keep the unit in question alive. Units that have more than one stack of multistrike like Animus of Will or Dante the Deceptive are ideal to stack up and capitalize on this bonus. Legion of Wax also benefits nicely, though the size increase can be a problem, especially when it starts splitting. If the above are not viable options, units with sweep can also capitalize on the bonus nicely.

    1.  History
See: Version History

## Ambient Charge
' is a Wurmkin  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
There is a large gap in the Wurmkin’s record of their history, an odd occurence for beings so intent on the preservation of knowledge. I’ve pieced together that during the Wurmkin’s rise to power, many Echoes were left unaccounted for, sealed away by the Kinhost in forgotten tunnels. Many that have been found have gone dark, their knowledge lost forever, but occasionally some are found that have fed off the passing souls above. It seems even this latent charge has been enough to preserve them.
    1.  Strategy
As  consumes a lot of cards in the deck, it is a card that can prepare an Infinite.

## Ancient Resonance
' is a Wurmkin  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
I could not Judge anymore, the simple scale laid out by the Divines too reductive for my beliefs. Souls are complicated, morally ambiguous beings. Hell and Heaven aren’t that different, each one having its faults, but also having its wonderful beauties. I am not proud of what I did, nor what came after. The only solace is that, at the time, I believed it to be right, and without that conviction we are all lost. Though, I doubt Seraph will see it the same way.

## Animus of Speed
' is a Banner Unit of the Awoken .

    1.  Lore
Newly formed, this Animus is no longer protected by its steel armor, making it quicker and deadlier. It is possessed by the soul of the Wildwood and the Channelers who guard it, often making my conversations with it obtuse and pointless.

    1.  Strategy
Animus of Speed is a very lackluster unit; it is a high-Attack, low-Health offensive unit that has no innate way of scaling itself or multiplying buffs applied to it. Quick, while a strong keyword, is not nearly enough when Animus of Speed only hits a single time; it is usually not even able to dent the backlines that would actually cause danger to the floor, which is what you would want Quick for. One may think that its Attack justifies the risk of using it, but unfortunately, that amount of power is nowhere near enough to clear even early-game frontline tanks, much less the high-Armor or Health tanks later on. Awoken even has access to Wildwood Tome as a Rare draft to directly give Quick to crucial units, making the positive aspects of Animus of Speed even more redundant.

Animus of Speed all but requires Multistrike and a Health upgrade to even be functional as a unit, and at that point, you might as well have taken any other one of Awoken's offensive units instead that offer significantly more than it with those same upgrades.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
| Multistrike is almost required on Animus of Speed for it to be useful, as having multiple hits on the unit somewhat justifies drafting it for its innate Quick, and allows it to take buffs better.
|-
| imageonly=y
| Animus of Speed requires Health buffs to not simply die to enemies with Spikes and Sweep, given its very low Health.
|}

    1.  Suggested Unit Essences (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)
Animus of Speed becomes better in the DLC as a consistency booster if your strategy requires Quick - not due to its own merits, but rather its essence, which provides Quick directly. If you see Multistrike (or otherwise the upgrade you want) for one of your damage units early on, then see Animus of Speed in a nearby Awoken Banner, you can draft the Animus of Speed for the infusion and finish upgrading your unit with something else without having to go to future Merchants of Steel, allowing you to get your main line up and running for potential upgrade overstacking from Concealed Caverns events.

Otherwise, Animus of Speed itself does not take any infusion particularly well that other offensive units would not simply take better. Even putting Multistrike onto it incurs a steep opportunity cost, as you can very often do the infusion the other way around and have that Multistrike unit take a free Quick while retaining its presumably superior base stats or attributes.

## Animus of Will
' is a Banner Unit of the Awoken .

    1.  Lore
These Animus have grown to see beyond their ring. Preternaturally aware of the actions of their enemies, they can predict their movements and strike accordingly. I've heard that they are rarely created by Channelers since their gift of sight can expose them to the reality of their imprisonment.

    1.  Strategy
Animus of Will is one of the Awoken clan's signature power hits, coming out of the gate with Multistrike 2. This makes it immediately a powerful recipient of any scaling you have drafted for your run, tripling the effectiveness of every Attack buff you put on it. It plays extremely well with in-clan buffs such as Rootseeds, Steel Enhancer, and Razorsharp Edge, and takes buffs from other clans just as well, especially Rage, which can quickly put this unit out of control. Thus, Animus of Will is a viable choice to be the main attacking unit in your run.

As with most aggressive units, Animus of Will struggles in terms of its survivability; this goes especially so for Animus of Will as it has extremely low base stats overall, starting at a paltry Health. Having a high-Health tank to protect this unit as it deals damage is crucial, especially ones that have secondary effects to scale other units on the floor, such as The Sentient on the Cultivating path or Awoken Hollow. You should also usually put survivability upgrades into this unit even if you have a tank on the floor, to protect it into Relentless and against Spikes or Sweep enemies.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

## Antumbra Morsel
' is a generated Umbra  Card in Monster Train.  It can't be obtained by any means outside of combat, but is instead the byproduct of certain Umbral cards.

This morsel is a common tier morsel making it one of the weaker set of morsels.  This morsel is most effectively used on allies with low base health or ones that scale attack on their own (like Overgorger or Awoken Hollow).

    1.  Lore
These precursors of the souls of Hell, called Antumbra, are fragments of the Umbra Shroud. Their friendly personalities make them my constant companions anytime I travel to the Crucible.

## Apex Imp
' is a Banner Unit of the Hellhorned .

    1.  Lore
As I have recorded at great length, the cycling of Imps to Demons and back again is an often observed phenomenon. Their existence in a sort of “transition state,” however, is rarely seen. These Imps, almost Demons, exhibit reserved behavior and a sort of misplaced confidence that I’ve only seen in human adolescents on their way to adulthood. I can understand the Hellhorned’s hesitance to make these Imps known...
    1.  Strategy
Apex Imp is one of the strongest Banner Units in the game, if not the strongest and most game-breaking unit overall. This is because Armor in the base game was simply not balanced around being able to also simultaneously offensively scale offensive units, allowing Apex Imp to very quickly get to outrageous numbers with very little investment. For example, playing a completely unupgraded March of Shields on Apex Imp is the equivalent of playing a double cost-reduced Ritual of Battle on any other unit. Seeing Apex Imp in an early banner with any Armor generation such as Welder Helper, Steelworker, or even Fortify all but guarantees a victory if the run is played properly.

The only challenge is to then find ways to multiply the impact of the Rage given to Apex Imp. Trample and Multistrike, as with most other DPS units, are key, as they allow Apex Imp to cleave through multiple enemies instead of wasting all of its accumulated Rage on overkilling just a single one. Duplicating the Apex Imp and finding floor-wide scaling solutions can also quickly put a floor out of control, especially with unit infusions in the DLC.

Imperialist Shardtail Queen is NOT recommended with Apex Imp, as unlike other Imps, Apex Imp wants to stay on the field for a long time and build up to massive amounts of Armor and Rage, not spawned in for its Summon effect then get killed after that, but Imperialist Shardtail Queen will just sacrifice Apex Imp anyways since it still counts as an Imp, letting all that built-up Armor and Rage go to waste.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

## Automatic Railspikes
' is a Clanless  Card in Monster Train.

Obtained from the Heph the Blacksmith Concealed Caverns event.

After getting 4 or more copies of the card in your deck, Heph comes back to replace the Slay ability with +10 Magic Power and Consume.

    1.  Lore
These railspikes can replicate themselves through technology I developed in the Crucible. They made the construction of the Rail a quick process. Some lubrication is needed to make sure that the spikes emerge easily, but oil has become hard to come by. Thankfully, brutal combat has allowed for other sources.

Unlike Clan-specific Railspikes, ' are not used to seal the covenant between the Rings of Hell, but instead are functional for the construction of the Rail.

## Awoken Hollow
' is a Banner Unit of the Awoken .

    1.  Lore
These souls, imprisoned in steel, try to break free before they lose their minds to the Wildwood. I had no idea of this until the Channelers told me. It took me some time to trust them again after that revelation.

    1.  Strategy
' is a great tank, entering battle with Health - though you will need healing spells to be able to take advantage of this, as it starts with just Health. ' additionally rewards healing spells with a Rejuvenate trigger for Cultivate 2 - strengthening the unit with the lowest current health with Attack/Health.

' enjoys the Restore that the Starter Deck has with Primary Awoken, as each instance of it being played will provide two Cultivate triggers over the course of the combat - one immediately, and the other at the end of each turn by building Regen. If it is alone on a floor, however, it can be quite passive because it takes up Capacity and does not have that much Attack. As such, it appreciates partners with force multipliers such as Multistrike or Sweep such as Animus of Will or Shattered Shell that take good advantage of its Cultivate ability to give its floor an offensive presence. ' is also great with Floor Overstacking, able to fortify its floor, making its partners less susceptible to Sweep themselves and slowly building them up.

When playing with Umbra, Lifesteal can also be very beneficial on it, as each of its own attacks will trigger its own Cultivate ability. Drafting Immortal Trade and waiting until the end of the combat to play it can all but secure Relentless phases in a very major way.

' is also a decent target for Adaptive Mutation thanks to its Health, letting it hit very hard in exchange for greatly reducing its bulk. Its Capacity and lack of innate Multistrike makes this difficult to pull off without specific support, however.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
| A simple Health is by far the best upgrade to put into Awoken Hollow. It will usually be on the front line, so giving it more Health will always be valuable. It additionally provides a buffer while you heal it up to its maximum potential health.
|-
| imageonly=y imageonly=y
| Other Health upgrades are fine in a pinch if you're looking to simply fill all upgrade slots, or cannot afford to path to another Merchant of Steel, though these are much worse than the Health boost.
|}

    1.  Suggested Unit Essences (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)
If you are able to support a Capacity floor, the best infusion for Awoken Hollow is to self-infuse it. This will give it a boost in stats while doubling its Cultivate triggers, resulting in it scaling its floor twice as fast. Against the increased difficulty of the DLC, this increased speed may be necessary if you lack density of other buff cards in your deck.

## Big Sludge
' is a Banner Unit of the Melting Remnant .

    1.  Lore
Even wax dripping has its limits. While it makes the target incredibly large and near unkillable, it also forces them into an eternal state of dripping. Wick or no, this compels them to continue to gutter more and more wax if their wish to retain this power.

    1.  Strategy

Big Sludge is a unit with very high damage potential, whose only drawbacks are its Ember cost, Capacity, and ramp up time. Big Sludge does scale extremely quickly from its Harvest ability; at an effective Attack per harvest, it scales roughly 3 times as quickly as Wickless Baron and is a fairly reliable way to deal with double heavy enemies when equipped with Multistrike. Obvious synergy with Remnant Host. It can be worth dedicating an entire floor to Big Sludge + chump blockers to ramp its damage as quickly as possible, followed by descending Big Sludge for relentless (Dripfall/Cave In), or stacking Stealth/Dazed for the boss. (Endless + Burnout Stealth tomb, Mortal Entrapment on Permafrost). Beyond taking Multistrike as an upgrade, the 2nd upgrade is often either Heartstone to make the unit more resilient against Spikes or against a boss, or a damage upgrade to assist with initial scaling. Largestone is generally not recommended, as the unit already has a high capacity that the player has to account for.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Multi-strike is the ideal upgrade for Big Sludge, as it allows the unit to start killing heavy enemies much quicker, as well as eventually start removing two heavy units all by itself.
|}

## Bog Chrysalis
' is a Banner Unit of the Wurmkin . It has Shell 5, and summons 2 Bog Flies when all Shell is removed. These bog flies inherit all upgrades and stat changes applied to the original egg.

    1.  Lore
The Wurmkin do not breed. At least, not anymore. Upon their decision to remain hidden, they created thousands of eggs, ready to hatch on command, eschewing the need for further reproduction. What a shame.
    1.  Strategy
Bog Chrysalis is an egg that hatches into two Bog Flies after it hatches that inherit the egg's stat changes and upgrades, effectively acting as two units in one. Since all upgrades and stat changes are applied to both bog flies after the egg hatches, their value is effectively doubled.

However, the above relies on one being able to actually hatch the egg. Thus, one needs to be able to remove 5 Shell while protecting the egg with Health at base. Hardened Hull helps directly in both tasks.

Bog Chrysalis fits very well behind Infector Spine Chief. The Infector's Strike ability generates charged echoes which not only helps to hatch the egg, but after that will also power up the Bog Flies' attack.  Especially against the boss's relentless, if the chief can survive a few turns, the flies' attack will be supercharged to a very high level.

Bog Chrysalis also works very well behind Corruptor Spine Chief. The Hatched Bog Flies represent 2 more attacks for the Chief to buff, and upgrading the Chrysalis' with Multstrike increases the attacks to 4, since the upgrade applies to both hatched units. Since the Bog Flies also scale with echoes themselves, they can scale their attacks to game winning levels.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Heartstone helps prevent the egg dying early, as it has Health.
|-
|
|Since upgrades apply to both hatched units, Frenzystone ends up representing 2 more strikes instead of 1, essentially applying twice.
|-
|
|Echo Stone gives both the egg and the flies an extra Attack per Echo which is doubly effective after the egg hatches.
|-
|
|By giving the unit Endless, you can now replay the egg after it hatches, allowing you to summon more Bog flies with just one egg. This is especially effective with Hardened Hull, reducing the echoes needed substantially.
|}
    1.  Pacts & Unit Essence (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)
Since all upgrades, including Unit Essences, pass down to the hatched units, Infusions with Summon effects happen 3 times total after the egg is hatched, and scaling effects happen to 2 units after hatching. Since Hatch effects kill the egg unit, Extinguish also trigger when the Chrysalis hatches.  However, do note that Status Effects applied after the Chrysalis is placed do not pass down, and anything including stat changes on the hatched units do not affect the egg itself.

## Bog Fly

## Bogdeep Cocoon
' is a Banner Unit of the Wurmkin .

    1.  Lore
In the places where the fleshy sludge has calcified, the Bogwurm’s Remains have concentrated and created new sentient life in its own image. Even in death, the Titans continue to save us.
    1.  Strategy
Bogdeep Cocoon is a very finicky unit to utilize in a run. Its steep cost of 12 requires significant investment just to hatch, and in the early- and even mid-game, it won't be a guarantee that you'll be able to activate it by the time the boss appears, depending on how well you can generate Charged Echoes. It also plays poorly with other Banner Units due to its high cost and Capacity, and unlike other Egg units, it is a poor user of size=20px due to how much Shell you need to remove to repeatedly hatch it, even if you have Hardened Hull. Thus, it is very hard to take speculatively, and should usually be avoided if you are unsure about the direction of your run.

However, if you're able to hatch Bogdeep Cocoon and support it properly with an Etch line, your reward will be one of the strongest units in the game. Bog Wurmling has Trample, monstrous base stats at Attack/Health, and gains a further Attack/Health on Etch. Once you've focused your deck down, it's likely that you won't need any other units to win the run, especially if you find high-impact Consume cards. Accelerated Incubation helps greatly in kickstarting the hatching process, especially with a size=20px. You will also want to look for Broken Memories and Wurmkin Etchings to generate Etch loops to continuously scale Bog Wurmling over the course of the combat.

The best use case for Bogdeep Cocoon is to pair it with Marshlord Echowright. Marshlord will automatically remove Shell from all Eggs on the floor on Etch, greatly speeding up the hatching process. The egg that Marshlord spawns on the floor can serve as a tank at all levels, giving you more time to hatch Bogdeep Cocoon and ensuring it has as much Health as possible into Relentless combat. At Level 3, Marshlord will spawn a second Bogdeep Cocoon which will scale alongside your Banner Unit copy, creating a devastating double-Trample floor that can easily win the remaining combats in the game, provided you can hatch both of them fast enough. Note that if you pursue this line, Bogdeep Cocoon should be the only Banner Unit in your deck, as if you fail to draw it on the first turn, you will not be able to play it with Echowright as the spawned egg will leave only Capacity on the floor.

## Bone Dog's Favor
' is a Clanless  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
As hellish as he is, Bone Dog isn't from this realm. The Awoken Channelers have traced him back to some unknown land ruled by one with no face. When we found him, we almost left him behind, but he's such a good boy.

    1.  Strategy
While  is a rare card, once its card is mastered, the alternative to purge becomes a very good choice and it can often be better to take the purge option. When score is important for a run, healing back pyre damage in a fight can however improve the player's ranking.

    1.  History
See: Version History

Originally Bone Dog's Favor was a rare drop from the Abandoned Train Event if the train does not collapse after the third card is obtained. As the chance for this event to happen was extremely low, many players finished the logbook and were missing only this card. This frustration led the game developers to add a new event in Herzal's Workshop increasing Bone Dog's frequency. The frequency was, in a later version and together with removal of the Halloween Cosmetics, decreased to what it is now.

Bone Dog is a reference to Shiny Shoe's previous game Death's Door.

## Bounty Stalker
' is a Banner Unit of the Melting Remnant .

    1.  Lore
Contracted assassins often employed by the Wickless to accomplish what even they cannot. Their incredible prowess in hunting Waxers has made them feared across the entire Ring, from Reformed to Wickless alike. I can't say I much like them myself.

    1.  Strategy
If Intent on Death is not available, it is important for  to die as often as possible for scaling, as such the earlier you draft  the more potent he can potentially become. Wickstone and Immortalstone is the fastest way to scale , but it is also a two edged sword as with only one Burnout it will not be able to do a lot of damage during relentless combat. Waxer Snuffer is an extremely-potent artifact with Bounty Stalker as it effectively doubles its Attack gain.

    1.  Pacts and Unit Essences
Draff is a particularly potent essence to infuse into Bounty Stalker, as this both gives it Multistrike which it really wants for better waveclear, and gives it Burnout letting it die and proc its Extinguish immediately without having to wait for it to get killed.

## Branded Warrior

Due to the slay trigger affecting all friendly units on the floor, it is very good in combination with Floor Overstacking by ascending or descending other friendly units.

! Description
|-
|
|Furystone is effective on units who already have a way of gaining Rage because of the way factorials mathematically progress/regress (pseudo-exponentially, with Rage decay/regressing at a rate of 1 per wave).
|-
|
|Strengthstone is effective on units with Slay affixes because, in order to benefit from as many Slay triggers as possible, you must make sure that the unit scores the Slay, which is further guaranteed by having as high an Attack value as possible.
|-
|
|Frenzystone is effective on units with Slay for the same reason Strengthstone is effective. Multistrike has the added bonus of the possibility of scoring two Slay triggers in one wave, because each strike could hypothetically score a Slay.
|-
|
|Speedstone is good assuming  already has the damage to constantly get Slay triggers. Since  will most likely not be the front unit, your other units will attack before he gets the chance to. If  attacks first and manages to get a Slay trigger, the Rage will be applied to all your Units on the floor and they will benefit from the Rage this turn, rather than next.

## Branding Rite
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
The focused application of heat on a Hellhorned hide will force it to calcify and provide a natural defense. Though I can tell you that the smell is truly...unique.

    1.  Strategy
' is a flexible utility spell that can be used either to fortify your units or kill back line enemies, depending on what the situation calls for - a unit with Health doesn't benefit from Armor, but a unit with Health very much does!

Its 5 damage lets it kill most relevant back line units, and its Pierce means that this will work even when Armor Emblem is in play. It can even kill Disciple Protector, though scaling up its damage to hit later-ring armored enemies requires upgrading its Magic Power, which isn't a strictly positive thing since it makes its defensive utility weaker.

It can also provide a net +15 to one of your own unit's longevity, provided they are able to survive the 5 damage. A particularly good target for this spell is Hornbreaker Prince's Wrathful line, which gains Rage from this card as well. Unfortunately, some frailer would-be targets, like Branded Warrior and Horned Warrior cannot take advantage of this card unless they are upgraded, so try to give them a Battlestone if this card is in your deck and there are situations where you may want to use it on them, such as against Sower of Sorrow.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Emberstone is fairly good on ', as it often finds a use, either to fortify one of your units or to pick off a backline enemy unit, and Emberstone mitigates its somewhat high Ember cost.
|-
|
|Powerstone is a possibility on ' in a pinch for the same reason that it is good on Horn Break - namely that it has Pierce and can pick off Health but high-armor enemies, but it also makes it worse at fortifying your own units. Horn Break is the better card for dealing Pierce damage on enemies if that is what you are using this for, but sometimes you have to take what you are offered.
|}

## Cave In
' is an Umbra  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
I've only seen the aftermath, but when a level of the Crucible collapses, very few Morsels survive. The Umbra don't even care to check for survivors, choosing instead to spawn new Morsels.

    1.  Strategy
Cave In is one of the worst cards in the game. This is because it lacks the flexibility of other movement cards, as it indiscriminately drops every unit on the floor, regardless of whether it is friend or foe. Movement cards that ascend or descend units are often used for Floor Overstacking, and it might seem like this card would be good at enabling this strategy. However, consider that cards such as Tiresome Climb and Dripfall can also disrupt enemy waves and send dangerous enemies away from the floor. Even Hidden Passage and Restoring Retreat are capable of doing this as well, despite their lack of Dazed application. Cave In, however, does not allow such flexibility as it still forces you to fight whatever wave your floor may be facing if you dare play it on your main floor. This can very frequently also overstack the enemy waves, sometimes even up to the 7-unit limit, allowing threatening backliners to slip past your defenses.

Some clans with no access to movement, most notably Wurmkin in the DLC due to their large units, can make decent use of Cave In to overstack floors if Umbra is acting as a supporting clan to your run. However, Umbra itself cannot take advantage of Cave In consistently. Generally, Umbra strategies want to set up on higher floors, as Umbra units scale by eating Morsels, which requires a turn and thus can be slow to come online. Umbra also does not benefit from usual overstacking strategies, as they want Capacity on the floor to play Morsels. Cave In is antithetical to both of these key facets of the clan's identity, both encouraging you to leave no room on the resulting floor and also forcing units down which you may want to be keeping on higher floors.

Theoretically, Cave In can be used to drop in Morsels from a higher floor into an Umbra unit. However, this is an incredibly inefficient use of resources that could be better handled by simply managing Capacity constrictions some other way and not drafting an incredibly situational card that costs Ember and actively hinders your line in the majority of situations.

Overall, despite having a few use cases, Cave In is a poor card that is rarely applicable to your run, and is often not worth drafting.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

## Champions
Your Champion is a card in your Starter Deck determined by the Primary Clan you choose before a run.

You will always draw your champion on the first turn of a Battle.

You cannot permanently duplicate your champion, and the only way to upgrade them is through the Dark Forge event found during Limbo, The Waxen Arches and Scorched Plains Rings.

Base Champions

Exiled Champions

The five Base Clan exiled Champions were added in the Friends and Foes (1.2) Update and the two Wurmkin Champions were included as part of the Last Divinity in 2.0

## Consumer of Crowns
' is a Banner Unit of the Hellhorned .

    1.  Lore
Truly a behemoth among beasts, this demon is the most decorated warrior in the Ring, having collected more horns than any living demon. It is highly respected amongst the Hellhorned and frequently avoided by me.

    1.  Strategy

' is very tempting with its 100 Attack, and you may think that ' is a great draft if your deck has a lot of imps. This is usually a mistake. In particular, it is a Priority Unit, which means it will frequently be drawn on Turn 1 when you have no Imps, resulting in you not being able to play it until much later in the fight and potentially depriving you of another Priority Unit that you would have drawn. A deck that takes it and invests important upgrades into it may draw it at the wrong time in a key fight and lose as a result.

For ' to be worth picking, you have to have multiple synergies with it - here are some common ones:
-  Imp-cicle
-  Pyre Chomper
-  Shardtail Queen, especially with the Imp parade upgrade tree
-  Improved Firebox (lets you play ' on turn 1)
-  The Unbroken Horn
-  Sketches of Salvation (it can play Imps and ')
-  A small deck (allowing you to redraw ' and still have a chance to win)
-  The Magic Hand mutator
-  (DLC) Cheater's Hand

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Frenzystone is effective on ', doubling its already impressive damage output from 100 to 200.
|-
|
|Speedstone is effective on ', patching up its weaker Health stat by killing enemies before they get to attack.
|-
|
|Heartstone and Largestone are effective on ' by raising its weaker Health stat. Largestone is notable on higher Covenant Ranks as it also lets ' hit some damage thresholds against enemies such as on Clipped Guardian and Steelwings.
|}

    1.  Suggested Unit Essences (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)

Since Multistrike and Quick are fantastic upgrades, Unit Essences that provide these effects are great, such as Animus of Speed, Animus of Will, Eel Gorgon, and Dante the Deceptive. If you have a hard time playing ' because you cannot manage to get enough Imps played by the time he is drawn, consider reducing his cost with a Pyre Chomper. Lastly, ' does have some survivability issues, and therefore would also benefit from Unit Essences from Railbeater, Husk Hermit, Crucible Collector, and even Lady of the House, assuming you have Burnout application.

Consumer of Crowns also has a potentially useful essence for powerful units with high Ember cost such as itself, Demon Fiend, Lodestone Totem, Shadowsiege, or First of Kin, allowing them to be much easier to play.

    1.  History
See: Version History

With the addition of Shardtail Queen and her starter card Queen's Impling in Friends and Foes, Consumer of Crowns has become a more playable unit.

## Crucible Collector
' is a Banner Unit of the Umbra .

    1.  Lore
These former wardens have left their post as an overseer of Morsel workers to become a collector of all variety of gems. They've found that ingesting the metals of the Crucible gives them strength they've never known otherwise. The rest of the Umbra Shroud, however, does not seem to appreciate their newfound independence as much as I do.

    1.  Strategy
Crucible Collector is one of the strongest lategame tank options in the game. With sufficient morsel support, it can be virtually invincible, tanking as many turns as it has Lifesteal charges. The biggest issue is making sure it doesn't die. In a pinch, you can place disposable units in front of it or give it Damage Shield to help it survive the early turns of a battle.

The card is considerably weaker against Seraph the Patient, who applies Melee Weakness to the front unit and deploys Attack minions, often resulting in the front unit taking a large (80+) amount of damage before getting to attack. If Seraph the Patient is your boss, consider using Crucible Warden instead, whose ability to get Damage Shield is much more effective against him.

Frenzystone is not recommended on ' as this will cause it to lose 2 stacks of Lifesteal per turn, hurting its longevity significantly.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
| Heartstone, Largestone, or other Health upgrades are an absolute must to ensure ' does not get killed before he can hit back and regain his missing Health. Attack upgrades can be good as well, but they are secondary to making sure he survives in the first place. Largestone does this quite effectively, though the Capacity hurts, considering ' wants to eat as many Morsels as possible.
|}

## Crucible Warden
' is a Banner Unit of the Umbra . It has a Gorge ability, meaning it can be used as a front line unit that consumes morsels.

    1.  Lore
The dreaded patrols of the Crucible Mines, wandering the long pathways in search of slacking Morsels and idle researchers. Though originally a mark of their lowly status, the Void that protrudes from their shadowed torso serves as a useful guard against worker uprisings.

    1.  Strategy
Generally, this unit is best used as a front line tank, due to its Gorge ability. This can be coupled with units that provide more morsels, such as a Morselmaker, or attackers with low health, such as an Animus of Will.

This unit can also be used on the 2nd or 3rd floor, since it can take down units that make it past the first floor, while still being able to build up damage shields. It's considerably effective to feed it Jeweler or Magma Morsels, due to their Eaten abilities providing Damage Shields or extra attack power respectively.

## Cycle of Life
' is an Awoken  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
When a soul is trapped by vines, the Wildwood gives it new life. And when the soul is released after the vines wilt away, that life is returned to the Wildwood and the others trapped within in.

    1.  Strategy
Cycle of Life is an incredibly inefficient card. Although the max. Health increase is valuable in a clan that can heal (and it is one of the only cards that can do so in the Awoken pool), its steep Ember cost does not justify the payoff. It is best compared with Echo Infusion, which gives the exact same amount of Health at Ember (but does require a Charged Echo to be on the floor). In essence, you are paying an extra Ember for 3, which is a completely unreasonable trade for such a low-impact effect. This is exacerbated in the DLC, as Spikes 3 barely scratches upgraded backliners, making that part of the effect usually completely irrelevant.

If you have the resources to reduce its cost, Cycle of Life can contribute positively to your run. Hollow units, and frontline tanks in general, appreciate the extra Health to stay topped up, particularly if you have Regen synergies or Unleash the Wildwood to make the Health increase go further. Due to the fact that it increases max. Health, it can keep Offering Monument perpetually topped up, and in that case it can be used for an infinite if your deck is small enough. However, all of this is a large amount of investment to simply make Cycle of Life playable. If you do not specifically need what this card does, you should generally avoid taking it and find other survivability solutions for your run.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|imageonly=y imageonly=y
|Cycle of Life is incredibly overcosted for the benefits it confers, and thus requires Ember reduction to be viable.
|-
|imageonly=y
|If you happen to find Cycle of Life early and run into the Abandoned Winged Concealed Caverns event, Cycle of Life is a good target to purge temporarily, as two out of three Heaven's Finest upgrades will bring its cost to Ember and make it much more playable.
|}

## Dante the Deceptive
' is a Clanless  Banner Unit.

    1.  Lore
Little is known of Dante, and what I do know I can't truly trust. While he seems to reign from Hell, he's made friends with many winged casting doubt on the place of his origin. But, he's always provided his help in the direst of times, so I will not question his allegiance.

    1.  Battle Quotes

- Dante, at your command!
- Dante, at your service!
- Instruct me as you see fit, my friend!
- My sincerest apologies for your imminent loss.
- Ah, I see you've noticed my candles.
- Oh, it's just a scratch.
- I'm sorry, did that hurt?
- Please, quit your yowling!
- I might be willing to trade sides...for a price.
- Is your train always so drab?
- Hold this for me, will you?
- Don't worry, you can trust me.
- You can't shake these old bones!

    1.  Strategy

' joins your train 2 rings after the Mysterious Figure event if you choose to accept the 3 Dante's Candle items. ' is a very strong unit that is capable of being the focal point of your run. Since you will always have at least 3 Blights when you receive him - the 3 Dante's Candle items that he gives you before joining - he will gain at least 3 Multistrike when played, and if you are playing on higher Covenant Ranks and start with Deadweights this will be higher - causing ' to strike enemies at least 6 times on Covenant Rank 25. When upgraded and given Attack buffs, this means s damage will quickly spiral out of control.

It is more worth taking Concealed Caverns events than usual with ', as the Ember Deposits and Penance Yard events are extremely beneficial with ' (and the Artifact you get with him, Dante's Cloak), as they give ' more Multistrike and those events cause Dante's Candle to be even more beneficial to your deck than they already are.

While ' can improve the deck significantly, choosing to take or not to take ' is usually more about the survivability of the player in the 2 Rings before getting Dante with additional Dante's Candle cards. Especially on high Covenant Ranks, taking on the candles can be a high risk.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Strengthstone and Largestone are very effective on units with Multistrike (especially units with a low default Attack value). The additional Attack Attack bonus of the Strengthstone for example is effectively applied multiple times on units with Multistrike.
|-
|
|Quick is very good on , as the unit can have an extremely high Damage output potential and therefor kill all units on the floor before they have a chance to attack.
|-
|
|Battlestone, similar to Strengthstone, is quite effective on units with Multistrike (especially units with a low default Attack value). Though the Battlestone provides less Attack, the additional Health Health helps protect low Health units from Sweep and other circumstances where the small additional survivability will be useful.
|}

## Demon Fiend
' is a Banner Unit of the Hellhorned

    1.  Lore
These demons, rumored to be the most ancient among the Hellhorned, do not speak in any known tongue. They don't appear to understand much, either, making any sort of relationship with them tricky to establish. Thankfully, they have a natural proclivity for fighting making communication unnecessary.

    1.  Strategy
Demon Fiend is overall a poor unit, and should be avoided in the vast majority of runs. While it has impressive base stats at Health / Attack, it has an incredibly restrictive Ember cost, preventing you from playing it under normal circumstances in the early-game. By default, you must take Fel's Remorse to be able to deploy this unit, which you will not have access to until you defeat the Ring 3 boss. Thus, when seen early in a unit draft or banner, it is often undraftable if your primary objective is to win the run.

Even when you meet the ideal conditions to draft Demon Fiend and then play it, the investment is often not worth the reward. Aside from taking up your entire turn to play even after taking extra Ember, it only offers its high stats, with no other inherent abilities to scale it or help it scale. Worst of all, it takes up Capacity on the floor, making it more difficult to put multiple units on its floor to take advantage of the Hellhorned's floor-wide scaling options, most notably Fledgling Imp. It is often preferable to draft and play offensive units with lower initial stats but far superior abilities, such as Horned Warrior and Railbeater, and scale them over time with Rage or other buffs while taking the generally superior Herzal's Compound to access and play these scaling mechanisms faster.

That being said, you will sometimes have runs where Demon Fiend is playable very early, such as if you see Forever Flame, Volatile Gauge, or Improved Firebox as your starting artifact. In those cases, Demon Fiend is still often not the best choice, but it is perfectly serviceable as your carry unit and essentially wins the early-game for free due to its frontloaded output, especially if you can find a size=20px. You must still work around the Capacity restriction, but if you know Demon Fiend will be your carry unit, you can make informed drafts earlier to support it - for example, focusing on Floor Overstacking rather than Imp strategies, and drafting targeted scaling cards such as Ritual of Battle to focus your resources on making it as strong as possible.

Demon Fiend is also particularly strong if you are shown Sketches of Salvation early. Sketches can easily cheat out Demon Fiend, allowing you to take advantage of its immense frontload value with no drawbacks whatsoever. This also makes a fully-upgraded Demon Fiend a particularly valuable duplication target.

Aside from these synergies, in the majority of cases, Demon Fiend will simply be a wasted slot in your Hellhorned banners or unit drafts - take this unit prospectively to the detriment of your own run.

## Devourer of Death
' is a Melting Remnant  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
The unfortunate souls who are unable to melt must instead feed off the deaths of those around them. They accumulate more energy as others souls are freed from their waxen forms, but they are doomed to never be able to free their own.

    1.  Strategy
Devourer of Death pairs well with Legion of Wax, as placing the Legion in front of the Devourer will cause at least 7 deaths, for an explosion of 105 damage, or more if the Legion kills anything before all units die. Similarly, though to a lesser extent, Remnant Host can provide Devourer with three deaths from Remnant Host itself and the two Draffs it summons. Even if the Draffs don't die in combat, they come with innate Burnout 1, and will die regardless after the combat round. Units with Endless can also be useful for feeding it, as can abilities to Reform units. Compounding this with the Waxer Snuffer artifact, which allows it to double its damage output, and the spell Intent on Death, which can more reliably trigger its ability when needed, without losing it in the process, can make this unit very effective at removing enemy threats and weakening bosses. Any damaging or other unit removal spell that can target friendly units can also be used to trigger its Extinguish ability when needed, but will necessitate some other methods of returning it to combat which could have debilitating drawbacks (see Additional Information below).

Taking full advantage of Devourer of Death can become difficult when faced with Trample, Sweep, and Spikes as these can prematurely kill it before it can build up enough Soul to be worthwhile. This can be made easier by increasing its Health Health with Upgrades, Infusions, or stat changes in combat, but doing so can become a balancing act of allowing it to survive long enough to build up Soul, but not so long that it can't die when necessary. This can be avoided by protecting it from Trample attacks on the bottom floor against The Last Divinity, and from Sweep on the top floor, but also by not increasing its Attack Attack power so it doesn't inadvertently kill itself on Spikes.

Alternatively, a potentially valid strategy might involve building Devourer's Health Health during combat so that it survives through to the boss's Relentless phase, allowing it to build up enough Soul to deal a significant amount of damage on death. Building its Attack Attack at the same time would help it kill enemies, further increasing its Extinguish damage. This could require significant resource allocation to Devourer of Death, however, which may be better used on another unit.

## Draff
' is a Melting Remnant  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
When wax has been melted and reapplied over and over again, it can turn into fragile resin. This makes the edges of a Waxer sharper and more dangerous, but much more brittle.

    1.  Strategy
' is a versatile attacker, able to deal high damage for a low cost, both in terms of Ember and Capacity. It is a good candidate for being Reformed for the same reason, able to be slotted in wherever needed for even higher damage and no Ember.

Cards like Molded, Primitive Mold, Rector Flicker's Dark Calling line, or especially Hallowed Halls (DLC only) can help you reform ' early and often, and Little Fade's Fire Light pairs excellently with its efficient Multistrike to capacity ratio. Hallowed Halls in particular makes excellent use of s Capacity and can put up to 6 ' on the same floor under the right circumstances with no capacity investment.

' does have one significant drawback that can make it hard to invest heavily into and rely on until your deck can draw through itself quickly - it is not a Banner Unit and therefore does not have the guarantees of being drawn early that they do. So if your deck needs it, you may fail to draw it in a key fight.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Since ' is very good at being Reformed, decks that do this well may well want to give ' another stack of Multistrike, as it can use it very well, and can be duplicated and paired with itself for a potent floor.
|-
|
|Strengthstone can give ' power early in a fight, hitting enemies for a total of 20 more damage..
|-
|
|Wickstone is effective on units who have the Burnout affix (assuming that it would be advantageous for the unit to live longer, which isn't always true). The +1 Burnout doubles the life time of the Draff, the Attack bonus is effectively doubled with Multistrike, and the Health bonus helps against units with Sweep.
|}

    1.  Pacts & Unit Essence (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)

' is one of the few characters that offers Multistrike in its infusion, with an additional effect that is arguably another bonus in the context of the Melting Remnant. As such, it should be considered whenever offered in The Last Divinity (DLC) for this reason alone. You can infuse it into a Banner Unit to negate its problem of sometimes not being drawn in a timely fashion.

That being said, infusing other characters into ' can be a worthy choice too, since it will make good use of any stats it gains (and scaling abilities that affect Attack and Health directly survive Reform), and can easily be duplicated and teamed up with itself once infused thanks to its small Capacity. Once its been duplicated and your deck is small enough, its lack of Banner Unit status becomes less important.

## Echoes of the Past
' is a Wurmkin  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
I cannot remember its entirety, but here are the pieces I’ve put together: in the beginning there were Three Divinities. Father, Mother, Child. They created the world for sport, to placate an idle Child needing distraction. Father created the structure of space and time while the Mother provided life as pieces. So, the Realm spun on for the Child, with life created and tossed aside just as plainly. Even the thinnest of these facts will drain the purpose out of the most willful; I understand the Wurmkin's decision to keep our meaningless creation a secret.
    1.  Strategy
    1.  Suggested Upgrades

## Echowright
' is the Exiled Champion  of the Wurmkin .

Their Starter Card is Echo Break.

    1.  Lore
I suppose it should be no surprise that the Shardsouls protecting the Exiles are one and the same as the Echoes found in the Wurmkin’s care. I’d understood the trapping of souls to be Talos’ idea, and that still seems accurate, but the transfer of knowledge back out of these shards was created millennia ago by now extinct Kinhost intelligence. Was the Echowright’s voluntary Exile a sort of last stand? Or part of something larger?

    1.  Battle Quotes

- Oh Divinities!
- Pew!
- NO!
- But echoes or crystals or charges or shards they're all the same thing and...ughhh where am I?
- Hehe...STOP...hehe
- A crack in the head is better than dead!
- Hehehe!
- Nononononono...
- Waiting, waiting, WAITING!
- WHOA!
- This isn't right.
- Boom!
- Hihihi hellooooooo.
- NO! Not again!
- Hurry pleeeease.
- Pew! Pew!
- It feels empty with Mother and Child now gone.

    1.  Upgrades

!
! style="min-width:100px" |
!

Resolve: Return 2 random consumed spells to your hand.

    1.  Strategy

    1.  The Shellsmith
With this path, look to set up ' on a crowded, powerful floor to get the most Armor per Etch. Bog Fly is a particularly notable companion because of their low capacity requirement and high offensive power with a good Charged Echoes generator, and ' in turn provides them with some staying power along with his own high Attack power.

If you cannot find a Bog Fly, Bog Chrysalis or other suitable companion, or even if you do and just want even more offensive firepower, consider cross-pathing with The Marshlord to get a Bog Chrysalis to put in front of ' to hatch into Bog Fly.

    1.  The Marshlord
The ability summons an egg in front of him, so be aware of that when placing. Note that you don't need to have Capacity to fit the egg - take advantage of this and place ' last on his floor to overstack the floor with his egg. Drafting another egg allows you to double-dip on his Etch ability, too. That being said, invest in Charged Echoes too - if eggs are your core strategy, you need them to hatch very quickly to avoid allowing the first few waves to waltz to your Pyre!

## Eel Gorgon
' is a Banner Unit of the Stygian Guard .

    1.  Lore
The Titan's name has been lost to time, the Titan now trapped and harmless. But the name's power has lingered within the Sea and seeped into the life of all those within it. Creatures that once listened only to the needs of their survival have now become intelligent guardians of the Ring. The Power of the Name has not faded, only found new owners. It is a cycle unlike I've seen elsewhere.
    1.  Strategy
Use stat buffs such as Rootseeds, Razorsharp Edge, and Steel Enhancer, as these will stick and not get cleared.

Keep in mind that, on the turn Eel Gorgon fights Relentless, its Multistrikes will last until the end of combat on its floor.  Thus it may be effective to save for example Frozen spells to play on that turn.

Emberdrain spells are very potent on Eel Gorgon. Immortal Trade and Perils of Production give bonuses that scale well with multistrike and the Emberdrain status will be removed at end of turn (along with the beneficial statuses).
    1.  Suggested Upgrades

Any pure stat increases are very good. As such Largestone can be good when the increased capacity does not lead to any problems.

    1.  Pacts & Unit Essence

' essence itself gives Multistrike for a rather small negative effect of higher cost of a unit.

The essence of Alpha Fiend onto ' is of particular interest as the scaling can extremely quickly get out of control. Besides that any pure Damage and Stat scaling is very good as such units like Siren of the Sea can provide a very good essence onto '. As pure stats are just very good on the unit, Demon Fiend or Shadowsiege can also be very good when enough capacity is available.

    1.  Additional Information

' cannot be upgraded with upgrades that give it Status Effects, since those would just be cleared between turns anyway. This is also the case for quick granted by Wyldenten, the effect gets cleared and not reapplied.

    1.  History

## Enflame
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
Deep within the Scorched Planes, Hellhorned Demons can sometimes be seen performing a "magma-walk" - a bizzare coming-of-age ritual involving full submersion into lava and a focused trek across the Plain. It's unbelievable that any Demons survive the ritual, but those that do are stronger for it.

 is a supporting card for Armor and Rage decks that rarely does define a deck, but often is a useful tool to enhance the Rage and/or Armor generation of a deck.

' is an Awoken  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
The Wildwood of Hell encases those that linger too long underneath its draping canopy. These living trees scare me more than I care to admit.

## Excavation Eruption
' is an Umbra  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
With little regard for their own lives, these Morsels seem to explode carelessly in the mines, paying no mind to the possible consequences of their actions. I've seen this backfire on more than one occasion.

    1.  Strategy
This spell works best against floors with a collection of weak enemies so that it can generate a large supply of embers. For the best effect, run this spell with the Stygian Gaurd clan as the other clan so that it can be used as fuel for more spell spamming and/or the cost can be reduced to a usable amount.

## Fledgling Imp
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
These impish Hellhorned have just molted into their new forms, as evidenced by their tiny wings and heavy tail. Their horns will continue to grow with each molting cycle, as well as their love of their volcanic homelands.

    1.  Strategy
Like most imps, Fledgling Imp is a low-cost, high-impact card that relies heavily on its Summon ability. It has natural synergies with cards like Imp-portant Work or Imp-olate that let you get additional value out of it after it is summoned. Fledgling Imp is one of the few sources of floor-wide Rage generation, making it a great addition to any Rage deck. Cards like Deranged Brute or Last Stand, alongside artifacts like Resonant Shard or Collection of Tails that increase the effectiveness of Rage, will amplify the power of Fledgling Imp.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

## Forgone Power
' is a Stygian Guard  Card in the Exile  Starter Deck. You begin with 3-5 of them depending on your Covenant Rank.

    1.  Lore
The Martyr speaks of the power a name has over the soul, that the Titan released its name for the good of Heaven, Hell, and Humans. Perhaps the same practice observed by the Stygian Guard is an evolution of this and grants them some minimal power as well.

    1.  Strategy
' is decent at whittling down frontline enemies and bosses early in the game. Later in the game, it loses potency, but still has decent synergy with Offering and Incant effects, especially with good card draw. It can remain a moderately useful card in the right deck. As such, especially on high Covenant Ranks, it can often be better to build a deck utilizing s strengths rather than spending resources purging it.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
' has no suggested upgrades, as none of the common upgrades can be applied to it, and the rare upgrades all have better targets.

    1.  History

## Forgotten Trade
' is a Wurmkin  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
Today I found it — my life, given up. It’s comforting, I suppose, to know that my mind isn’t truly lost, but the truth I’ve found, of mistakes and guilt, are a consolation not suited for the offense. Exiles sacrificed at my behest. Judgement forsaken to save human souls. Titans destroyed by my hand. All ending in the loss of our Realm. As a Titan, a Judge even, I was supposed to make no error, yet I doomed the very ground upon which I walk. What do I know of judgement...

## Fortify
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
Leather is a common material among the Hellhorned, so I've lined my garb with the material for protection. Given the stench, I'm sure the source of it is better left a mystery.

    1.  Strategy

' is a fairly low-impact, low-risk, easy-to-use card that provides a little bit of extra defense for a unit. In most combats, it should simply be applied to a front line unit that needs to be able to take more hits.

One particular strength of ' is its ability to easily strengthen frail back-line units in situations where they may otherwise be in danger, whether due to Sweep or Spikes. (That being said, this card often doesn't provide enough defense to warrant taking Retribution as a Trial if you are relying on frail back-line units to stay alive to win.) Hornbreaker Prince (especially its Reaper line), Horned Warrior, and Branded Warrior are examples of units that appreciate this.

Generally, decks that want to draft this card are ones that appreciate this strength, or simply have a lot of card draw.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

None. None of the common upgrades can be applied to this card, and the rare upgrades generally do not have enough impact to justify their cost.

## Frozen Lance
' is a Stygian Guard  Card in the Base  Starter Deck. You begin with 3-5 of them depending on your Covenant Rank.

    1.  Lore

An ancient relic of a time before the Covenant. When I first came to the Stygian Sea, every resident of the Ring carried one to aid in their defense of the reigning Titan. There still lies some magic within the surrounding ice.

    1.  Strategy

' is a very weak starter card. 6 damage, in the context of front line enemies, is not a lot of damage, even early in the game. It is therefore a very high purge priority. Even Surgestone, while improving its damage output, does not make it a good card later in the game - the main benefit of that upgrade is that it allows you to get rid of it more easily.

It does trigger Incant abilities which are common on the Stygian Guard, and does not require a target to be cast, which can push it just below Train Steward in terms of purge priority.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|' is a primary target for removal, and Spell Railspike and Purgestone alike can help accelerate that process. However, depending on how many Spells you have in your deck, and whether or not you are fighting Seraph the Diligent, you may want to conserve copies of ' with either of these upgrades, as you'll be able to use them once during that fight, and proceed into the fight against The Last Divinity with these cards removed.
|-
|
|Surgestone is effective on ' as it raises its damage to 26 and gives it Consume, getting it out of the way of other, stronger cards after use. This upgrade is particularly helpful in the early rounds if your Ring 8 boss is Seraph the Diligent, as you may not want to remove Spells from your deck and risk your stronger Spells being Consumed each turn.
|}

## Furnace Tap
' is an Umbra  Card in Monster Train. It is a consumable spell in the Umbra clan.

    1.  Lore

Much to my dismay, the Umbra are able to withstand the heat output from the furnaces on the Boneshaker, allowing them to commune with its power and use it for themselves - at the cost of energy to the train.

    1.  Strategy
' is very powerful, but its Emberdrain 4 debuff will cripple you for future turns, realistically letting you play just Ember spells on future turns, unless the unit ' was applied to dies.

Perils of Production has incredible synergy with this card. On turns after you play this card, if you draw Perils of Production, you can play Perils of Production after Emberdrain has drained your Ember and get Ember to play other cards - since Emberdrain cannot give you negative Ember, this will work no matter how much Emberdrain you accumulate on your units. The fact that Perils of Production's Rage goes well with Multistrike increases their synergy all the more.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Freezestone is good with  as it lets you save it until the boss appears, letting you use its benefit in the most important moment of the fight and not need to worry about its crippling downside, all while only needing to draw it once during the fight.
|-
|
|Stackstone doubles the Multistrike given by this card, making it even more potent. It also doubles the Emberdrain to 8, so this upgrade requires you to have a plan to mitigate this downside, such as Freezestone or a Perils of Production with Holdover.
|-
|
|If you have a Perils of Production with Holdover and a small deck, Eternalstone can be what it takes for your deck to go nuclear, giving a unit lots of both Multistrike and Rage. Giving this card a Keepstone can make this even more ridiculous if the stars align.
|}

## Glimmer
' is an Awoken  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
If struck by a surge of electricity, a Sapwood will crystallize into glass. The sap within will become preserved as a supercharged healing solvent. But I can't seem to break the glass without losing much of this sap in the process.

    1.  Strategy
Glimmer is a useful card that can stay relevant throughout the whole run. Early on, its AoE damage allows you to wipe out squishy waves such as Clergyman rushes as well as access other threatening backlines. Later on in the run, it is an excellent recipient of any Magic Power upgrades; a simple +10 pre-DLC allows it to clear backlines into the lategame, and in the DLC, Divine Temple Magic Power upgrades can make Glimmer an obscenely high-impact card that is even worth duplicating at Hellvents.

Glimmer's healing effect, while often secondary to its AoE damage, can also be helpful to top up your units. It has good synergy with Awoken's Rejuvenate lines, most notably acting as a huge immediate heal for Awoken Hollow and Thorned Hollow that also powers up the floor, but generally most clans appreciate the extra security it can provide.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y imageonly=y
|Extremestone in the DLC puts Glimmer above many important damage thresholds, allowing it to kill even upgraded backline enemies as well as provide a massive heal to your floor. If you find this upgrade, Keepstone becomes a very strong second upgrade, keeping your floor healthy every turn while solving backline access for the run.
|-
| imageonly=y
|Alternatively, you can opt to put a Truestone on Glimmer to give it Piercing, allowing it to crush enemies with Spell Shield and making Ancient Hate trials much easier. Truestone by itself is generally not as impactful as Extremestone, however - ideally, you want to combine the two for maximum effect.
|-
| imageonly=y
|If not playing with the DLC, Powerstone allows Glimmer to kill all backline units in the game.
|}

    1.  History
See: Version History

!Version

|Added Glimmer.

## Glugsider
' is a Banner Unit of the Wurmkin .

    1.  Lore
These sludge behemoths have crawled through the Bogs for eons, absorbing and digesting whatever remained trapped. I pride myself on being observant, so imagine the sting to my ego when I found these piles of filth feign stupidity to keep the Wurmkin and their cairns hidden.
    1.  Strategy
Glugsider is an awful unit, and is one of the worst Banner Units in Wurmkin, if not the game altogether. Its Ember cost is completely offset by its summoning requirement of Extract 4, meaning you essentially need full Charged Echoes in a room to deploy it. Because Glugsider is a Banner Unit, you will see it in the first few turns of the combat, where it is unlikely that you will have the resources to charge a floor this quickly. To deploy Glugsider consistently, you are almost required to have specifically a Soul Siphon with size=20px - an Uncommon card that you will likely only see after you draft this unit.

Deploying Glugsider, however, is only half the battle. Its Revenge trigger - applying Reap to attacking enemies - requires that you have Charged Echoes on the floor for that Reap to deal any immediate damage. However, due to its summoning requirement, Glugsider will remove Echoes from the floor when it is played, meaning that in order to get any value out of it, you must charge the floor again. This is an unreasonable amount of investment for the vast majority of runs that could be better served using these resources to scale the stats of more consistent Banner Units such as Keeper of Echoes and Shardsoul Carver. You could attempt to place one of these units alongside Glugsider to keep up with scaling, but this is usually impossible as the final nail in the coffin - this unit's Capacity - rears its ugly head, forcing you to find a Capacity solution to assemble such a floor.

Theoretically, Glugsider can act as a sturdy tank and backline solve for a Sketches of Salvation run, as with Sketches, you can bypass the grueling process of getting it into play. However, Sketches of Salvation can spawn Glugsider at the back of the floor where it will be completely useless, requiring that you have access to a card that can move units to the front of the floor, such as Vine Grasp or March of Shields. This means that hilariously, you will still need further specific support for it even with an artifact that can cheat it out.

Outside of this, Glugsider will rarely help your strategy. Getting it into play requires that you warp your deck around it, and its merits fall far from winning you the run on its own, which would be the level of payoff required to justify its steep, anti-synergistic deployment requirements. Thus, it should almost always be avoided in any serious run where you do not see Sketches of Salvation.

## Hoarfrost Effigy
' is a Stygian Guard  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
Even before the freeze, the granite monuments of the Titan dotting the sea were cold structures. Now they’ve become permanently frozen fixtures of the seascape, ominous presences easily recognizable by their sharp profiles.

    1.  Strategy
Hoarfrost Effigy is one of the worst spells in the game. This is because its design is completely antithetical to what makes Frostbite a strong status - its waveclearing ability. In a given run, you often apply large amounts of Frostbite at once to dense waves with cards such as Titan Sentry or Coldchannel Solgard, killing the high-damage backlines while significantly softening up the tanks in front for your units and Pyre to finish off and thus significantly decreasing the number of hits that your main floor must inflict to prevent leaking enemies into the Pyre.

Hoarfrost Effigy is completely antithetical to this playstyle, encouraging stacking all of the Frostbite on a single unit. This is arguably only useful in flying boss combats, and is inconsistent even in those combats. Not only does this rely on the boss lining up with a floor that you have inflicted massive Frostbite onto already on a turn where you have drawn this card, but chances are that the first play of this card will not even be that impactful as only the tanks would be left alive even if that given wave had backline units to apply Frostbite to. You would have to find a way to play this card multiple times while trying to also make sure the waves that you removed Frostbite from don't walk up to your Pyre with impunity.

Additionally, as a Rare card, it is very unlikely that you see Hoarfrost Effigy before already defeating the Ring 3 Major Boss, further limiting the number of combats that it can be legitimately helpful against.

Even if the stars align on your Frostbite run, it is very likely that if you were able to apply a critical mass of Frostbite to enemies in a boss combat, you would inflict enough of it onto the boss naturally to win Relentless anyway. Thus, Hoarfrost Effigy is often overkill and relegated to flying boss kill achievements or score hunting, and it is not even the fastest or most consistent strategy at doing so when the Stygian Guard has access to Spell Weakness synergies to one-shot bosses on low Covenant Ranks.

While Hoarfrost Effigy was already a situational card pre-DLC, it has become even worse with the DLC as The Last Divinity has Purify to purge all Frostbite from itself at the end of the turn. This makes the card completely non-viable for runs reaching the Divinity, as it will be completely useless in the most important fight of the run.

## Horn Break
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
A defeated opponent's horns are broken as a ritualistic act of dominance. The horns are then collected by the victor as a trophy.

    1.  Strategy
' is a consistent way to remove a backline enemy of your choosing for the whole game, even without an upgrade, and even when the Armor Emblem is active, with the additional benefit of being able to kill some armor-based heavy enemies such as Disciple Protector, Clipped Defender, and Steelwings, though killing the latter two will require an upgrade. It's a solid card that often finds a way to be useful. The difficulty with drafting ' is that Torch performs a fairly similar function, albeit not as well. As such, if you want to keep your deck small, upgrading Torch is an alternative to drafting it. This is not as much of an issue with the Exile starter deck, where ' has much less competition.

In The Last Divinity (DLC), with Pact Shards, killing armor-based heavy units becomes a lot less feasible, as their health generally becomes too high to be killed by this card (though an Extremestone can help with this in some cases). However, ' remains useful as a way to pick off backline units, even Shard-enhanced ones, as long as it is upgraded — and unlike its rivals, it does not need a Truestone to hit many crucial Shard-enhanced targets with Damage Shield or Spell Shield, such as Winged Conduits or Conduit Redirector, or backliners protected by Clipped Reflector.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Powerstone is a very important upgrade on '. ' is at its best against Disciple Protector, Clipped Defender, and Steelwings, who have low health respectively, and a lot of armor, which this card bypasses thanks to Pierce. Since few other sources of damage have Pierce, and so other damage will have to go through the enemy's Armor, Pierce will be wasted if it doesn't KO the enemy by itself in most cases. As such, it is very crucial to power up this card by Ring 4, when Clipped Defender starts appearing, who is not KOed by this card without Magic Power.
|}

## Hornbreaker Prince
' is the Original Champion  of the Hellhorned .

His Starter Card is Torch.

    1.  Lore
Cursed with no horns, the only son of Grrrg has taken to collecting the horns of his opponents, dispelling any doubt of his fighting prowess and convincing me of his rightful place as champion of the Hellhorned.

    1.  Battle Quotes

- For the Hellhorned!
- For Grrrg!
- It's a shame the Winged have no horns.
- Once Seraph is defeated, I'm leaving this train.
- What did you say about my horns?
- No being should have to live in this steel deathtrap.
- I'm not fond of this cold...
- Raaargh!
- You want to hear where I got these horns?
- Too quick for you?
- You will make a fine trophy!
- Hellhorned, rally to me!
- I could always use another trophy!
- It's gonna be a bumpy ride!
- My horns are sharp!
- You will fear the Hellhorned!
- Maybe I'll start collecting wings.
- My horns are quick!
- Soon we will return to the Plains.

    1.  Strategy

    1.  Brawler

Hornbreaker Princes Brawler line gets a lot of Multistrike, giving it high damage potential. It's up to your deck to take advantage of this.

The most immediate problem with the Brawler line is its lack of initial power and lack of ability to scale that power on its own - its damage is the lowest of the three upgrade lines, even with Multistrike taken into account, and while its inital Armor is nice, it isn't sturdy enough to serve as a great tank. However, it is a lot easier for Hellhorned cards to scale Attack than it is for them to scale Multistrike, which gives this line a lot of potential. Cards that give Rage such as Fledgling Imp, Rage Serum, Ritual of Battle, and effects that amplify Rage like Resonant Shard, Collection of Tails, or Deranged Brute are what you are looking for.

Taking this upgrade line in Limbo on high Covenant Ranks is dangerous unless you either already have some ways to scale its Attack or have a strong start in general, but it is capable of getting very strong.

Taking upgrades in either the Reaper or the Brawler line later may be worth it if you haven't found great ways to scale its Attack or find yourself really needing the roles those lines fill, but given how strong Multistrike 4 can be with the right support, this can feel like a missed opportunity.

    1.  Reaper

s Reaper line hits hard from the start of the fight and gets stronger with every kill, capable of amassing enough Attack to kill bosses in just a few turns with the right support.

That said, it may require the most support of the three upgrade lines.

## Horned Warrior
' is a Banner Unit of the Hellhorned

    1.  Lore
Though slightly unwieldy due to the recent loss of their tails, these maturing demons have grown into their horns, making them valuable soldiers of the Hellhorned and of our Rail.

    1.  Strategy

', with Multistrike taken into account, has one of the highest base initial damage values (30) among all non-Champion monsters of any clan; all units that hit harder initially without upgrades have difficult play or usage requirements.

As such, ' is a simple, but effective unit. Early in the game, it pulls a lot of weight on its own with its powerful attacks while being no more difficult to play than a Train Steward. However, like most units, it isn't capable of taking on later ring combats by itself, even with upgrades. With Multistrike 1, it's also fairly good at taking advantage of Attack buffs from Rage or other sources.

' is a great target for Floor Overstacking.
