## Husk Hermit
' is a Banner Unit of the Awoken .

    1.  Lore
Using Wildwood sap to expedite the growth of their vines, these Animus inhabit the remains of their armor. Their larger vines allow them to strike multiple enemies at once, while still being able to retreat into their shell if necessary.

    1.  Strategy
Husk Hermit is the simplest Sweep unit in the game, coming in with high base stats with no other text on its card. It was originally designed to serve dual-duty, as a frontline tank with respectable health that can clear backline units with its Sweep while the unit behind deals with the high-HP tanks in front. In this role, Husk Hermit performs fine in the pre-DLC, although Health is certainly not quite enough for a single floor late-game setup and requires some support.

However, the true power of Husk Hermit is its ability to take buffs and force multipliers just as well as every Sweep unit in the game - even the offensive ones like Shattered Shell - without necessarily requiring survivability upgrades. Sweep is an incredibly strong keyword in Monster Train regardless of what unit says "Sweep" on the card, for its ability to deal damage to an entire floor at once; this means that you immediately solve the question of having enough hits on the floor to defeat the densest waves of enemies, only requiring your line to be fast enough to scale up to the first tanks. Husk Hermit is thus best used in a purely offensive manner as your main damaging unit behind another tank; with Multistrike or Trample, it can be buffed up to a critical mass to wipe out entire floors at once. This is especially true in the DLC, where Animus of Will can provide a Multistrike infusion into it, making accessing Multistrike significantly more consistent.

While Husk Hermit lacks other effects that the other Sweep units have, its base Health, while not enough for it as a frontline, is a luxury on a backline meant to deal tons of damage. Health makes it much easier to play cards such as Razorsharp Edge on it without any fear of putting Husk Hermit too low, and also sometimes allows you to be greedy with your Merchant of Steel drafts, holding out for powerful upgrades such as size=20px and size=20px rather than having to take survivability to address Spikes and Sweep enemies or bosses.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

' is a Stygian Guard  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore

When Hell was frozen, the Stygian Sea was cursed with an eternal storm, making my travel across it near impossible. Fortunately, I was later saved by the Guard, but the invaders of Hell were not so lucky.

## Icy Cilophyte
' is a Banner Unit of the Stygian Guard .

    1.  Lore
Even further communion with the ice has allowed these Sirens to master its freezing nature. This leads me to believe that the Stygian may be the only clan that has benefited from the Freeze.

    1.  Strategy
Icy Cilophyte is inarguably the worst Sweep unit in Monster Train. It possesses paltry base stats of Attack / Health, the lowest among all Sweepers, and has no in-clan way of boosting its Attack outside of unit infusions in the DLC, as the Stygian Guard scales strength exclusively through Incants. It struggles to make any sort of impact on wave-clearing and Relentless alike without cross-clan help and hitting extremely critical upgrades at Merchants of Steel.

The intended way to play this unit seems to have been to use it on a lower floor behind a tank, softening up enemies by applying Spell Weakness to them to then blast them with powerful spells as they attempt to ascend the train. However, this is an incredibly inconsistent strategy at high Covenant Ranks as the line has no security into Relentless phases; if you have a separate Relentless strategy on a higher floor, using Icy Cilophyte this way spreads your Banner Unit draws too thin for no immediate impact, potentially causing you to lose control of the combat within the first few turns. If you instead use Icy Cilophyte how you would use other Sweep units - as a recipient for buffs and force multipliers to deal heavy damage to dense waves - the Spell Weakness applied is completely irrelevant.

That being said, the fact that this unit has Sweep at all automatically makes it worth considering, even if it does have poor base attributes. If you have the upgrades and buffs to make any Sweeper win you the run, Icy Cilophyte will get you there all the same.

## Immortal Trade
' is an Umbra  Card in Monster Train. It is a spell in the Umbra clan.

    1.  Lore

An unfortunate outcome of our invention, Void consumption has become an addictive practice amongst the Umbra. When that no longer satiates their hunger, the entire replacement of self with these Void shards becomes the only way to gain any satisfaction.

    1.  Strategy

' gives a unit 4 Lifesteal but inflicting the Emberdrain effect on that unit, crippling your Ember on future turns unless the unit dies. It can help a powerful unit stay alive in the right context, but at a cost of Ember over the next 2 turns (assuming no other Emberdrain effects).

If you do not have any synergies with ', it can sometimes be worth enduring the Emberdrain effect and playing it anyway, as 4 Lifesteal is quite good on Penumbra, especially on its Monstrous upgrade line, and other Attack and Health units. Unlike Perils of Production and Void Binding, it isn't possible to meaningfully play ' on a unit that you plan to kill before next turn except against a boss, so if you cannot build around this card's strengths and weaknesses it is a high removal priority.

This card is at its best when you are able to manage the Emberdrain debuff by building around it and other Emberdrain cards, either using Perils of Production or by aggressively reducing the cost of this card and other cards in the deck to Ember. The fact that it itself costs Ember without upgrades makes it very easy to fit into those decks.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Stackstone can be a good upgrade on ' provided you are building around it and other Emberdrain cards, causing it to give 8 Lifesteal. An Emberstone pairs well with this upgrade to get the Ember cost back to 0, especially if you are creating a deck with mostly Ember spells to get around Emberdrain's downside.
|-
|
|Freezestone can be a solid upgrade on ', as you may not want to play it on turn 1 or 2 before you've played all your units (in the Ember spell archetype) or drawn your Holdover Perils of Production (in that archetype). In addition, even in a deck that isn't building around Emberdrain, this upgrade can be useful, allowing you to play it just as the boss appears for up to 4 more turns of survivability, or 8 with Stackstone. Being able to play it when it's time to is worthwhile.
|-
|
|Keepstone can be a good upgrade on ' in a deck that aims to have mostly Ember spells and play Emberdrain cards at will, as playing it every turn is quite strong, and having at least one card every turn that costs Ember improves your deck's consistency. It can be good on a Holdover Perils of Production deck too, but be wary that 2 cards with Holdover will make your draw through other cards quite slow.
|}

## Inferno
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
The Scorched Planes are known for frequent firestorms that can rain down at any moment, striking anyone unlucky enough to be caught below. This has made my travels through it particularly short.

    1.  Strategy
Inferno is a very high-impact spell and can single-handedly solve your waveclear for a run. This is due to the fact that it deals massive Piercing damage, ignoring Spell Shield, Damage Shield, and Armor when played, which lets it heavily damage almost every enemy unit in a wave (if not outright one-shot them), allowing your units on another floor to easily pick them off. Its high cost can easily be mitigated by visiting a Merchant of Magic and applying size=20px upgrades to it to make it more playable, with its powerful effect being well worth the investment.

The main drawback, the fact that it also damages one's own units, can be mitigated by simply playing the spell on a floor empty on the player's side. Alternatively, this card can be used to clear out friendly units, whether to free up Capacity, activate Extinguish effects, or to replay said units with Endless or Reform.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y imageonly=y
|Inferno is often either a really good card or a really bad card depending on your train layout, and if it's a good card, you want to play it every time you draw it. Bringing down its otherwise difficult-to-afford cost lets you do that.
|-
| imageonly=y
|Inferno can be really strong if you can play it every turn on a floor, decimating most enemies without putting your units in harm's way with correct placement. However, this upgrade requires support to work because of the high Ember cost of this card, and applying Keepstone means you can't apply two size=20pxs. Artifacts such as Split Anvil, Lightstone Casing, or taking Fel's Remorse, can support this strategy. In the DLC, this strategy is much more consistent as you can apply size=20px to Inferno, allowing you to much more easily hold it over without needing Artifact support.
|}

## Legion of Wax
' is a Banner Unit of the Melting Remnant .

    1.  Lore
When Waxers are reduced to almost nothing, but still cling to what life is left within, they will sometimes band together for protection. This creates a new form that is collectively controlled by this collection of dying Waxers.

    1.  Strategy

Taking the Waxer Snuffer artifact allows ' to last an incredibly long time, and to create what is effectively a damage buffer. This synergizes awkwardly with Burnout if you Reform the Legion, as half of your buffered units will not form due to the 7 unit limit in rooms. However, this can also be beneficial for clearing rooms and for ensuring maximum damage output towards a group of enemies.

The Resin Block artifact also synergizes well with ' (assuming you can Reform it), as the stat changes apply to its children too. This can maximize the damage output of your Legion, provided you can kill its first stage, and clear the floors again fast enough. Wicked Blaze is a good choice to Reform it for similar reasons.

If you have the Capacity and the necessary cards, you may also want to place it behind a tankier unit and kill it, and potentially the Twins of Wax as well, yourself. This can allow you to maximize the effectiveness of any damage boosts without the child creatures being exposed directly to attacks. A Subsuming Blade that has killed one unit deals enough damage to kill an unupgraded ', leaving any Armor or Damage Shield intact to be inherited by the Twins of Wax, and will get a permanent damage boost when doing so.

## Little Fade
' is the Exiled Champion  of the Melting Remnant .

Her Starter Card is Primitive Mold.

    1.  Lore
For all that I've learned of these Exiles, it should be no surprise to me that the Exile of the Melting Remnant is a thieving child. My visions with her have been brief, but she looks to be free of the wax for which her Ring is known. Perhaps her sacrifice, and the sacrifice of each Exile, granted immortality to the clans.

    1.  Battle Quotes

- Who you callin' Little, featherface?
- Come a little closer, I want to show you me pointy friend!
- Come here, you!
- Me knife's mighty sharp, wanna see?
- This metal box ain't containing Fade for long, you'll see!
- I was swiping feathers off your grandpappies backside before you were even a twinkle in his eye!
- Now where's that ol' faker Flicker at these days, eh?
- What are you so angry about?
- You best watch your mouth!
- Me fingers are light, best be careful!
- Little in body, large in brains, I always say!
- You best mind your manners 'round your elders!
- Careful or I'll knick ya!
- All this dripping wax is making me stomach all tumbly.
- Oi! You go any o' them shinies lying about?
- Oh come on, I won't hurt you that much!

    1.  Upgrades

    1.  Strategy
Although she can be difficult to play in the hands of newer players, Little Fade is one of the strongest Champions in the game, and arguably the strongest overall. This is due to the fact that all three of her paths have incredible late-game carry potential with run-defining power. Between the incredibly quick floor-wide scaling of Fire Light, the frontline- and Relentless-crushing power of Eternal Flame, and the snowball potential of Little Icarus, there is no Little Fade path that cannot win given correct play. She also comes packed with Primitive Molds, which immediately opens up Reform and Burnout strategies from the very start of the game regardless of the path chosen, not only for herself but for other units in the Melting Remnant clan.

## Lodestone Totem
' is a Banner Unit of the Stygian Guard .

    1.  Lore

Of the totems that the founding Guard built, the Lodestones are possibly the most important. They provide energy for the Stygian Guard by siphoning power from those around them using some ancient technology that's eluded my understanding.

    1.  Strategy
Lodestone Totem is an extremely powerful and lucrative unit, yet can be a difficult one to pilot. Provided that your Incants are fast enough, the amount of Sap you apply can solve waveclear by itself by debilitating entire waves, causing otherwise dangerous enemies to walk up to the Pyre room with an effective Attack and simply do nothing as the Pyre kills them. Two copies of Lodestone Totem on the same floor can be especially deadly and become a win condition even into the lategame, but even one of them behind a scaling unit can take an immense amount of pressure off your floor.

There are several challenges to using Lodestone Totem as your main line. The most glaring flaw with the unit is its steep Ember cost, forcing you to find a way to manage it lest you simply be too slow to get going. Unless you have incredible highrolls in your run already such as Flicker's Liquor or Forever Flame to be able to play multiple copies of Lodestone Totem across two or more turns, it is strongly recommended to take  Fel's Remorse as your Ring 3 boss gem if you identify that this will be your main carry unit. This is especially true on high Covenant Ranks, as setting up on top floor (which is recommended for Incant units in general) will incur Emberdrain on your units, thus actually preventing you from playing a second copy of Lodestone Totem on turn 2.

The other issue with Lodestone Totem is addressing Relentless phases against bosses. While loading up bosses with Sap buys you many turns, you still need some method of offense to be able to leverage those turns to actually defeat the boss. This is especially true in minor rings where you only have one turn to apply Sap, which might not be enough. Scaling your Champion on the floor, or incorporating a Siren of the Sea or Nameless Siren on the floor in some way, can be critical towards solving this issue.

With these issues addressed, Lodestone Totem can easily win you the run, and can even trivialize the entire Divinity combat on its own.

## Memento Mori
' is a Melting Remnant  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
In memory, a Waxer is never truly dead. Through a process we developed together, their faces are embossed on deadly weapons and serve as a reminder of what once was and what will be.

    1.  Strategy
Memento Mori is a very weak spell, being incredibly overcosted at Ember for how slowly its damage effect scales. It is incredibly difficult to generate enough unit deaths to get this card's damage up to a critical mass except on the most dedicated Harvest lines featuring heavy use of cards such as Votivary and Remnant Host. While this support can get Memento Mori up to respectable numbers, it is very often the case that if you can achieve that much death on your floor, your Harvest units are likely already winning you the run without its help.

Compared to analogues from other clans such as Ancient Synergy or Battering Ram, Memento Mori falls far behind. Even in-clan, it is overall outclassed by Crushing Demise in terms of immediate output and efficiency at removing high-HP enemies from the combat. Overall, the reward of Memento Mori is not worth the investment and it should very often be skipped or even removed from your deck if it shows up in your opener.

## Morsel-Made
' is a Banner Unit of the Umbra .

    1.  Lore
As the mines of the Crucible twist farther into the depths of Hell, Morsels find themselves safe from the Shroud in their solitude. In the furthest reaches of the Crucible, the Morsels have banded together to create singular entities that further increases their collective chances of survival.
    1.  Strategy
Morsel-Made is a run-warping unit that can dramatically shift your strategy, as it does not require any floor space for Morsels beyond the Capacity needed to play them in the first place, nor a turn to eat Morsels at all. Its Gorge ability is fairly mediocre on paper, but because you can feed it as many Morsels as you can make on a single turn, decks that aggressively produce Morsels can scale it incredibly quickly. As such, it is one of the few Umbra units that can consistently perform in the DLC, essentially ignoring all of the clan's weaknesses.

The sooner you see Morsel-Made in a run, the better, as you have more time to adapt your drafts and cultivate your deck for it. Focus on generating large quantities of Morsels; cards such as Packed Morsels, Antumbra Assault, and Retch, which produce multiple Morsels from a single low-cost play, are highly valuable. Grovel and Gem Trove, while more expensive, are also excellent pickups to support this unit. Morselmaker and Morselmaster are worth considering slotting on the floor as well to further increase the number of Morsels eaten per turn.

Morsel-Made pairs better with Primordium as a Champion, as it can quickly eat Primordium and gain its stats within a turn or two, while it can compete with non-Architect variants of Penumbra for either Capacity or Morsels. However, Shadesplitter is a significantly better starting card than Plink for Morsel-Made, as Plink falls off significantly later on in the game and is inconsistent with its Morsel generation. Keep in mind the strengths and weaknesses of the Primary v.s. Exile versions of the clan when building your line around this unit.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
| Frenzystone is critical for Morsel-Made. In any deck optimized for it, Morsel-Made will be a scaling powerhouse expected to deal and take most of the damage for the entire run. Thus, giving it Multistrike allows it to put its high stats to better use and kill multiple enemies per wave.
|-
| imageonly=y
| Shieldstone is superior as a survivability upgrade to other usual alternatives, as Morsel-Made will be able to dramatically increase its stats throughout the course of a battle. 3 Damage Shield provides a large amount of security at the start of combat while Morsel-Made still has low stats, protecting it just long enough before it becomes functionally invincible.
|}

## Morselmaker
' is a Banner Unit of the Umbra .

    1.  Lore

Neither Umbra nor Antumbra, these in-between shades roam the mines as independent troublemakers, often splitting their own Morsels to undo the work done in the Crucible. I’ve been unable to decipher any sort of motive other than spite.

    1.  Strategy
' can be a potent defender if you also have a Winged Technology or Chain of Gems. When placed in the front, the two Morsels it summons can block at least four hits per turn, with any left over increasing the s stats, for just Capacity, which makes it especially good on the 2nd floor on Covenant Ranks 20+. Upgrades that increase Health or give it Endless are very effective with this strategy. Though this strategy can work without those artifacts, it's significantly less effective. You'll also need to scale the back unit with some other means that isn't Morsels.

## Morselmaster
' is a Banner Unit of the Umbra .

    1.  Lore
These Umbra rogues no longer have the need to create new Morsels from their own flesh. Instead, they attract existing ones to their nefarious calling by disrupting their connection to the hivemind. I have little other information on these creatures as I prefer not to spend more time with them than necessary.

    1.  Strategy
Morselmaster is one of the worst Banner Units in the game. Its passive effect to duplicate Morsels on the floor is only applicable for a narrow range of strategies, and ultimately isn't even that good for those strategies in practice since paradoxically, it performs worse when your Morsel generation is strong.

Most Gorge units take up Capacity, and with Morselmaster itself taking up Capacity, this leaves you at Capacity left to play Morsels on most floors - except Morselmaster will immediately fill up the remaining floor space when you do so, making the other Morsel-generating cards in your hand bricks. This means that you need a way to increase Capacity on the floor as soon as possible, such as with Architect Penumbra or (in the DLC) a Space Prism with size=20px, or take Light of Seraph after Ring 3 or 6, all for a minuscule benefit that can easily be solved simply by increasing the density of Morsel generation in your deck. As it is a Banner Unit, Morselmaster can also take draw priority away from the carry unit that you actually want to feed these Morsels to, which can actually slow down your scaling in the first few turns.

As this is something you want to do anyway if you have a strong Gorge carry, Morselmaster quickly becomes irrelevant, and the Capacity it takes up might even be better used to place an extra Morsel where it would've stood. If you must use Morselmaster to duplicate Morsels on your floor, try to use high-quality Morsels, such as Morsel Miner from Making of a Morsel, to make its effect more worth the investment.

All of these problems become exacerbated in the DLC, where Umbra as a whole is considered the least consistent clan due to The Last Divinity's Sweep on top floor (which is where Umbra ideally wants to set up) putting significant pressure on Morsel-based strategies. The Divinity fight requires fast scaling and deployment of key units as soon as possible, something that Morselmaster can actively hinder you in. With only a baseline of Health, even if you find a solution to play on top floor, Morselmaster will not survive long against the Divinity's Sweep.

## Nameless Siren
' is a Banner Unit of the Stygian Guard .

    1.  Lore

Those that enter the resting place of the Titan rarely emerge. But those that do are permanently imbued with some of its power, relinquishing their names as part of this new communion. While I've tried to wring an account from them on their time with the Titan, their minds seem to have expelled any contact with this Unnamed power.

    1.  Strategy

' allows you to convert your ability to play many spells into a high attack power. In contrast to Siren of the Sea, s scaling concentrates on offense at the expense of defense. So she will hit harder, allowing you to defeat Heavy and Armor class more easily with her Incant, but you will need a way to protect her from simply getting killed by enemies.

One way to do this is to simply put a defensive character in front of her. Good candidates are those who have Incant abilities themselves, such as Guard of the Unnamed, Titan Sentry, and Solgard the Martyr.

Guardian Stone or Lodestone Totem can also be effective partners to give ' some longevity - Guardian Stone in particular can help against enemy Sweep or Spikes.

As with any Incant ability, Founding Seal is a great find if ' is a key piece of your strategy.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
| Speedstone is effective on units who can amass high attack values such as ', allowing their offensive prowess to translate to defense by simply killing enemies before they get to attack.
|-
|
| Heartstone/Largestone is effective on ' to help her from dying too early in a fight. Many of her defensive supports require support themselves, and she can take some time to get going. More Health can often buy you a turn to get set up.
|-
|
| Runestone is effective on units who have the Incant affix. You will already want to regularly cast your spells on a particular floor (with your Incant units). Having Runestone on a unit (especially front line units) with Incant will gain an additional bonus. Though Nameless Siren isn't a front line unit, during the Relentless phases of the boss fight, the Armor provided by Runestone still allows her to attack more times and deal more damage.
|-
|
| Frenzystone is effective on units with the potential to obtain a high Attack value, effectively double the damage dealt (which could mean the death of two enemies, rather than one). Doubling a value of 100 Attack is far more effective than doubling 10 Attack.
|}

    1.  Pacts & Unit Essence (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)

## Nexus Spike
' is a Clanless  Card in Monster Train. It is a Clanless card that can be acquired from a Concealed Caverns event that allows the player to combine the effects of two spells in their deck. Unlike with unit fusion, the original spells remain in the deck after the event.

    1.  Lore
These Divinities don’t exist on any physical plane known to Heaven or Hell, but their power leaks from their existence to ours as brief flickers of light. With each Divinity felled, those flickers became more static, and now the last remaining Divinity holds our Realm hostage for my mistakes.

Mechanically and lorewise, ' seems to be separate from the Railspikes in that it is not an X Cost Ember spell and was not a Railspike created by a clan to celebrate the pact of the Covenant
    1.  Strategy

As  has some odd interactions for cards that are meant to be applied to only enemies or only friendly units, some cards can be used in unexpected ways:

- Burnout cards such as Wicklash, Purifying Cleanse can target enemies and will kill enemies when burnout runs out.
- Fragile works on enemies, making Onehorn's Tome a potential offensive spell.
- Shroud Mitosis can duplicate any unit, as targeting requirements get removed. However, Shroud Mitosis needs to be the second spell added. If it is the first spell added to nexus Spike, the Morsel requirement is not lifted.
- Adaptive Mutation works on enemies. As such this could be used against Mini-Bosses in The Last Divinity: Divine Intervention
- Perils of Production can be used on enemy units that you kill in the same turn to prevent Emberdrain.
- Petty Theft can be used to generate money from friendly units.

-  Combining AOE and front enemy unit spells:
  -  Spells that affect friendly as well as enemy units, such as Glimmer, Inferno, affect all units on the floor. Combining this with scaling damage cards that are not targeted like Bramble Lash, Battering Ram, Memento Mori,  Echo Break overwrite AOE damage from other spells. For example, apply Glimmer and Bramble Lash to deal Bramble Lash on every enemy for the Glimmer damage and then again Bramble Lash damage.

Some interactions that can not be as easily exploited:

- Restoration Detonation does not do damage to enemy units after healing, but to friendly units.
- Mortal Entrapment does not lose its already-damaged requirement.

As  costs Ember, it is often good to copy very expensive spells to get the added benefit of a card without the extra cost.

Upgrades are added to both effects, so two damage spells can benefit from the same Magic Power Upgrade or Status Effects from the Doublestack Upgrade.

    1.  Mechanics

I created signposts for the offering sites across the seafloor. But my shoddy work has made them vulnerable to the energy from the Unnamed below, destabilizing them entirely.

' is a strong card draw enabler that should be considered whenever offered. ## Offering Token
' is a Stygian Guard  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore

Making offerings to the Unnamed Titan has become a religious experience among the Stygian Guard. These offerings are made with such frequency that I helped to create tokens to represent a Guard’s offering to the Titan and simplify the process.

    1.  Strategy
Offering Token has two major functions: a free, controlled way of discarding Offering cards in order to play them for free, as well as a 0-cost way of activating Incant.

When not used to discard an Offering card, Offering Token has the potential downside of making your hand worse by forcing you to discard the worst card in it. In the case that the worst card is useless (whether it's a Deadweight or simply a card you wouldn't have played anyway), it has no downside. But in cases where you otherwise would have gotten value from every card in your hand, Offering Token hurts your draw.

That said, it is the easiest, most reliable option for activating Offering. Given Holdover, it can guarantee you that you can discard an Offering card every turn for little-to-no cost and without slowing down (or speeding up) the rate at which you cycle through your deck while also providing an extra Incant activation on any floor you want.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

## Onehorn's Tome
' is a Hellhorned  Card and a Tome in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
"Hell's Horns, History of he Hellhorned," by Grrrg Onehorn. The terrible handwriting of the imp scribes has made this tome an excruciating read and something I don't plan on returning to soon.

    1.  Strategy

Multistrike is an extremely powerful status effect on units that have high Attack or a potent Strike/Slay effect, but the Fragile effect is a significant downside. Even when placed behind a tank, a Fragile unit will die instantly to any enemy with Sweep or Spikes unless it has enough Armor or stacks of Damage Shield to avoid losing health.

' is one of the few ways to get Multistrike on a Champion. In particular, Hornbreaker Prince's Wrathful line benefits from Onehorn's Tome immensely, thanks to it gaining Armor with every Slay. Extra attacks mean more opportunities to gain Armor; three extra Slays from the Multistrike will always grant more Armor than the HP "lost" to the Fragile debuff, allowing him to survive longer while also doubling his damage output.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

|-

|-
|
|Stackstone is very potent on ', as this means you will get two additional strikes out of a single spell cast. Keep in mind that the Ember increase needs to be prepared for.
|}

## Overgorger
' is a Banner Unit of the Umbra .

    1.  Lore
A seasoned collector of Antumbra Morsels to the detriment of its own well-being. I've learned to steer clear of these creatures, as each new morsel they eat is a danger to all around them.

    1.  Strategy

' is a run-defining unit that can amass a large amount of Attack over the course of a run in the right deck. It can exceed Attack if well-fed, letting it kill any enemy in one hit and be a serious threat to bosses.

There are some obstacles you will need to overcome to achieve this, however. It is an early-game liability, especially on high Covenant Ranks, and its initial weakness coupled with its Ember play cost mean you may take some Pyre damage in the next couple of fights. It may be worth declining Trials in early Rings if you take ', counting on its strength later to make up for the lost value. s Health does not scale, and it can only Gorge if it is in front, so it will be vulnerable to being killed. Sacrificing Morsels to protect it is possible, but very much not ideal. Putting it on the 3rd floor and defensive upgrades help with this. Its Capacity combined with its hunger for Morsels means it is quite difficult to put other fighting units with it - a Cave In and other floor-changing effects when the boss comes can help, especially if they put ' behind other units, which is good because of its relative frailty. It can only eat 2 Morsels per turn on a normal Capacity room, so a way to increase Capacity, such as Space Prism or Seraph's Light, or ways for it to eat Morsels prior to turn end such as Feast or Shroud Spike help a lot.

On later Rings, ' can be put behind other units, particularly if it has Attack, as at that point it can kill any enemy, and its ability to kill bosses will largely hinge on whether it can survive long enough against them rather than further increasing its Attack. Investing in Capacity will also pay off at this point as you will very much want to duplicate ' with Hellvents.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
| Frenzystone doubles the potency of ', often letting it kill two heavy enemies at once late-game and making it twice as effective against bosses.
|-
|
| Heartstone helps with s longevity, which does not scale over the course of the game. Since ' has to be in front in order to Gorge, this is quite helpful. Shieldstone has a similar benefit.
|-
|
| Quickstone is another way to help with s longevity once it has several Gorges under its belt, letting it kill an enemy (or two, with Frenzystone) before it gets to attack.
|}

    1.  Pacts & Unit Essence (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)
If you are lucky enough to find an ' and a Morsel-Made on the same run, you can fuse the latter to the ' to scale up the s Attack as often as you can summon Morsels each turn, removing its dependency on Capacity.

## Paraffin Enforcer
' is a Banner Unit of the Melting Remnant .

    1.  Lore
Once a footsoldier has been promoted to an Enforcer, they are no longer considered the punching bags of the Wickless Remains. But that doesn't mean that they won't haze new recruits the same way they were.

    1.  Strategy
The strength of Paraffin Enforcer depends on whether you are playing with The Last Divinity (DLC) enabled or disabled. In the base game, Paraffin Enforcer is somewhat awkward and finds it difficult to fit on lines. Its reliance on Multistrike can be a serious issue, since it is necessary to speed up this unit's scaling - Rage 3 to the floor per turn simply isn't enough for a unit that takes up Capacity. Even if you find Multistrike for it, unless you can find a way to also apply Burnout to it quickly enough, it will not scale itself, and you must quickly extend this Burnout as well lest all of the accumulated Rage go to waste. Thus, Paraffin Enforcer tends to be a below-average unit that requires multiple pieces of your run to come together to make it work, unless you have Fire Light Little Fade to reliably anchor the line.

However, Paraffin Enforcer gains a tremendous power boost in the DLC, to the point where it is arguably the best Melting Remnant banner unit, significantly ahead of nearly every other except for possibly Bounty Stalker in its run-carrying potential. Multiple additions in the DLC work in its favor, most notably the Unit Infusion mechanic - you can now infuse other units into Paraffin Enforcer to provide Multistrike or Burnout as needed, or even give its infusion to other units, where it confers its entire scaling effect plus Burnout 2. Additionally, the Divine Temple size=20px upgrade can allow you to draw key Burnout extenders on the very first turn, greatly increasing the consistency of obtaining and maintaining Burnout on either Enforcer itself or whatever it's infused into. This gives Paraffin Enforcer tremendous flexibility in how it can be played, allowing it to act as either your main carry unit or an extremely strong cross-clan infusion into a Multistrike or Sweep unit.

While it does still require some investment to get Paraffin Enforcer online, the benefits far outweigh the drawbacks, and it is a strong choice in the DLC, being able to carry your run at all stages of the game.

## Penumbra
' is the Original Champion  of the Umbra .

Its Starter Card is Shadesplitter.

    1.  Lore
I have no hard facts on its origin, but it is rumored to be the beginning of the Shroud itself, formed in the long forgotten depths of the Crucible and giving life to the rest of Hell. This would make it the first being in Hell. It has yet to confirm or deny my questioning on the matter.

    1.  Battle Quotes

- You will be banished!
- I'd much rather return to my Crucible.
- Return to nothing!
- You will suffer our ancient power!
- Begone from our Ring!
- Hell has come for you.
- Nothingness is everything.

- Silence will fall again.
- Begone from our realm!
- I am the first and will be the last.
- It used to all be so much...quieter.
- We consume all!
- You Winged are out of your depth.
- BE CONSUMED!

    1.  Strategy
Penumbra has strong base stats and paths that serve a wide variety of purposes, from being a supportive unit to potentially being the focal point of your run. With Architect fitting into strategies that appreciate having the equivalent of a free Intrinsic Space Prism, Monstrous being a huge stat stick that can either carry by itself or simply soften up waves for higher floors, and Glutton scalingHealth andAttack rapidly for clean Relentless wins as well as incredible frontline damage, all of Penumbra's paths can provide great value to your run. On the latter two paths, the biggest problem to solve isCapacity on Penumbra's floor; at Capacity (and even higher in the Monstrous path), Penumbra can only be fed 2 Morsels by default, encouraging you to draft many of Umbra'sCapacity increasing cards or take a Light of Seraph if necessary to maximize its potential.

However, this changes significantly in the DLC as Umbra is widely considered to be the worst clan at high Covenant Ranks, requiring very specific solutions to address the Last Divinity fight that shuts down many of Umbra's usual strategies. While Penumbra is still strong in its own right, and can still support a winning run well, it is dragged down significantly by being tied to Umbra; its carry paths are especially devalued as their stats were balanced around the base game, and now require significant amounts of support to function as they used to.

## Perils of Production
' is an Umbra  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore

To power the Constructs of the Crucible, the Umbra Shroud feeds them excavated fuel. If fed mined Ember, the furnaces go into overdrive, working doubly hard at the cost of even more Ember. This discovery was crucial in the construction of the furnaces within the Boneshaker.

    1.  Strategy
' gives you Ember and Rage now at the expense of Emberdrain that will drain your Ember next turn - if the target of ' lives. As such, it is often a good idea to target Morsel units with this spell, as if they are Eaten you will not suffer the Ember loss next turn.

' can be used as a temporary Ember booster to go with expensive or X Cost-cost cards, though it can be rather weak if not aligned with them.

Alternatively, it can be given a Keepstone or thrown into a Fissure to allow it to be reliably drawn on most or every turn, allowing powerful synergies with other Emberdrain cards.

Not only is this a cheap source of Rage on a unit, it enables you to play other powerful cards that inflict Emberdrain on your units as a drawback while not compromising your ability to play spells later, such as Immortal Trade, Furnace Tap, and Void Binding.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
|Keepstone can be a run-defining upgrade on '. Once ' is drawn, play it every turn, and you will still have Ember each turn after playing this card, since the Emberdrain debuff it causes triggers at the start of your turn, before cards are played, and no matter how much Emberdrain is on your units, your Ember cannot be drained below 0. With several copies of this card with Holdover, you can gain far more than Ember while scaling your units all the while, even allowing you to play cards such as Shadowsiege, though be mindful that this limits you to 3 cards per turn without extra card draw.
|-
| imageonly=y
| Twinstone increases the efficiency of ' in a single card draw, reducing the need to duplicate the card (but also paradoxically making duplicating it much more enticing). It doubles the Rage gain to 6 and gives a net Ember, though beware that playing both copies will also give you 4 Emberdrain as well. Twinstone is often combined with Keepstone to offset this weakness, with this combination on ' being nearly run-winning by itself.
|-
| imageonly=y
| Putting Intrinsic on ' can greatly enhance your first turn by giving you essentially Ember to play with. Keepstone also combines well with it, as you can start the Emberdrain card spam as soon as the combat starts.
|-
| imageonly=y
|Freezestone can be effective on ' in some decks, making it easier to line it up with a Ember or X Cost-cost card or on a turn after Immortal Trade, Furnace Tap, and Void Binding where you need Ember in a pinch. The latter scenario is quite relevant if you are going for a mostly Ember deck with Emberdrain cards, but a key card does not cost Ember.
|}

## Petrified Skull
' is a Clanless  Card in Monster Train. It is one of the cards that can be obtained in Armageddon Battlefield. If this card is still in your deck after 2 battles, you will obtain the Hope for Peace artifact, giving it Consume instead of Purge.

    1.  Lore
Through my time here, I've learned that Hell's resolve is unmatched. Even with the Pyre extinguished, the last shard provides a glimmer of hope to defend this horrible treachery.

    1.  Strategy
' is a very strong spell in just about any deck, removing a unit of your choice.

You should avoid using the spell before you obtain the Hope for Peace artifact so that you will be able to use it for the rest of the run, if there is any way to avoid doing so.

Note that in the The Last Divinity: Divine Intervention (The Last Divinity (DLC) only) fight, this spell is very potent - it can remove the Ring 1 and Ring 2 bosses that do not have Relentless that appear in waves 4 and 7, one of the major threats in that fight.

## Plink
' is an Umbra  Card in the Exile  Starter Deck. You begin with 3-5 of them depending on your Covenant Rank.

    1.  Lore

The Primordium does not speak through words but through floods of feeling. The bounds of its mind seem endless. It’s no wonder that the ultimate split of the Primordium created a varying number of personalities, not the least of which are my friendly Morsels.

    1.  Strategy

' is a strong early card in fights where there are lots of Health enemies, frequently killing 2 enemies and generating 2 morsels against waves of Clergymen. However, Health enemies are not common past Ring 4, and not all battles before that will have many good targets for this spell. Taking the  Armor Emblem trial will make ' a lot less effective as well, often even if it is upgraded. On the other hand, ' is quite effective on early turns if Mark of Invasion is taken.

Without either a Surgestone, Powerstone, or some other source of Magic Power, this spell is therefore a high purge priority.

|Powerstone is also an option on ', giving it 11 power and allowing it to continue having a chance of killing backliners and generating morsels. It can be better than Surgestone if you'd rather ' not to consume on use.

## Primordium
' is the Exiled Champion  of the Umbra .

Its Starter Card is Plink.

    1.  Lore

I can feel the end of my days loom near as these visions become more frequent and more confusing. It seems even the Umbra Shroud has origins beyond my prior knowledge. This Primordial shadow rumbles with the weight of the entire Shroud under its cloak. But what happened to this ancient creature?

    1.  Battle Quotes

- ....sharrrrdtail...
- ...sooooolgaaaaard...
- ...wyldennnnntennnnn...
- ...faaaaaaadeeee...
- ...taaaaalooooos...
- ...aaaaaarchusssss...
- ...we...the...exilessssss...
- ...protect...sacrifice...failllllll...
- ...failllll...
- ...stuck...loop...
- ...we...are...sssshhhhhhadooooow...
- ...we...are...oneeee...
- ...I...am...maaaaanyyy...
- ...die...
- ...DIE...

    1.  Upgrades
{| class="wikitable center"
!
!
!
!
|-
! Superfood
| Health

Eaten: Add this unit's , , and status effects to the eater.
| Health

Damage Shield 2.

Eaten: Add this unit's , , and status effects to the eater.
| Health

Damage Shield 4.

Eaten: Add this unit's , , and status effects to the eater.
|-
! Aggressive Edible
| Attack
| Attack
| Attack
|-
! Stalwart Snack
| Buffet 5.
| Buffet 8.
| Buffet 12.
|}

    1.  Strategy
Primordium is often considered one of the strongest Champions in the game, even in the DLC (where Umbra is widely considered the weakest clan at high Covenant Ranks). Because it passes on all of its stats when eaten, any Attack or Health increases you apply to it (or even any buffs in general on the Superfood path) will be amplified by a factor of whatever its current Buffet stat is. This makes your scaling hyper-efficient, provided Primordium survives to be fully eaten, and allows you to create monstrous solo-carry units that can mow down entire combats by themselves. The main challenges of a Primordium run are thus to find ways to prop it up quickly, and find an appropriate unit to feed the buffs into. Units with Multistrike and/or Sweep are often the best recipients for the incoming stats, since any Attack increase will be further amplified by the number of hits the unit can dish out.

When Primordium passes on its stats, it does not include any temporary modifications from buffs such as Rage or debuffs such as Sap (though the status effects themselves can be passed on with the Superfood upgrade). Also, Primordium passes on its maximum Health regardless of any damage it has taken.

As Primordium is not a Morsel, Shroud Spike cannot target it. Feast works on Primordium as normal.

If the allied Clan is Melting Remnant, note that Primordium can not be Reformed if it dies after being Eaten, only if it dies from other means before its last Buffet is used up; as such, it can be beneficial to intentionally kill Primordium before it runs out of Buffet and is eaten for good, either through targeted damage spells such as Subsuming Blade (this will scale its damage too!) or through spells that kill your own units, such as Cannibalize or Hallowed Halls.

## Pyre Chomper
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
These imps have taken a rather strong liking to the Pyre as a snack food, making them horrible pests aboard my train. Though the sharp crystals don't digest well...

    1.  Strategy
Pyre Chomper is a low opportunity-cost unit that provides a net gain of Ember when played, taking into account the Ember needed to put it into play. Drawing a Pyre Chomper often means that you can play out the rest of your hand for the turn, allowing for high-impact plays. It also has good synergy with cards and artifacts that provide a payoff for the big surge in Ember, such as X Cost cards and The Unbroken Horn.

The most common use of Pyre Chomper as an integral part of your main line is fueling an Endless Transcendimp. One of the downsides of Transcendimp is its high Ember cost, making it difficult to play every turn; however, once you have activated the Summon ability of Pyre Chomper even once, playing Transcendimp will result in a net gain of at least Ember instead of a loss.

Overall, Pyre Chomper is usually not a bad pickup; it provides notable immediate output just by itself, and can open you up to extremely powerful synergies if the cards fall in the right place.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
|As with most Imps, Endless is a worthwhile consideration, allowing Pyre Chomper to die every turn (potentially tanking hits for you) and come back to continue applying its Summon ability. With Pyre Chomper in particular, however, you would only really want to summon it every turn if you have a particularly expensive deck, as the Ember gain provides no immediate benefit to your units compared to other Imps such as Fledgling Imp or Welder Helper, and may not be worth having to redraw Pyre Chomper over other cards.
|}
    1.  Suggested Unit Essences (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)
It is generally recommended to not involve Pyre Chomper in any unit infusions. If you started with Pyre Chomper in your opening cards (on Covenant Ranks 1 and up), and are at a Ring 2 Merchant of Steel with no other Imps, it may occasionally be worth it to "bank" an size=20px into Pyre Chomper to then infuse a more impactful Imp, such as Fledgling Imp or Welder Helper, into it later. Outside of this specific situation, however, since both of those aforementioned Imps are Common cards, you will not see them at all in rewards after Ring 3, which is where you would usually start to see Pyre Chomper. Because you are very unlikely to see Pyre Chomper first in any given run, this infusion direction remains an edge case.

## Queen's Impling
' is a Hellhorned  Card in the Exile  Starter Deck. You begin with 3-5 of them depending on your Covenant Rank.

    1.  Lore
In these dreams, the Shardtail has shown me the result of her Exodus: the first cycling imps in the Scorched Plains. I've never encountered these sharded imps in Hell, but their resemblance to those I have met is uncanny. Perhaps these dreams are something more...
    1.  Strategy
Queen's Impling, when not played with their intended Champion Shardtail Queen, is one of the worst starter cards in the game. This is due to the fact that they require Capacity and Ember to play, and can be hard to get rid of because of this, not to mention that their inevitable deaths can even proc enemy Harvest or Slay triggers if improperly played.

Even when they are played, their Summon effect only deals 10 damage to the frontline enemy. This is a very low-impact effect with limited utility in comparison to Torch, which can snipe dangerous backlines, self-trigger Revenge effects, remove more powerful Endless Imps from your floor to replay them, and reach Collectors in the early-game. Torches can also take size=20px at magic shops to amplify their power in the late-game, while Queen's Implings cannot take unit upgrades meaningfully in the same way, nor can their on-Summon effect be amplified as easily. Their only real benefit going into the late-game is that if you manage to play them out, they leave the deck for the combat, making your redraws cleaner.

With Shardtail Queen, the value of Queen's Impling increases as they give powerful secondary effects to her when played on the floor, whether it's being tossed with Imperialist for AoE damage, being played for free with Imp Parade, or increasing her Attack with Royalty.

## Railbeater
' is a Banner Unit of the Hellhorned

    1.  Lore
These Hellhorned are some of the most respected blacksmiths of Hell from spending several cycles on steelwork. Not to mention, they're a fantastic help in the construction of our Rail.

    1.  Strategy

' serves as both a tank and utility unit, using its Armor to tank hits and its Strike ability to inflict Melee Weakness on struck units.

' is an excellent partner for units with high Attack values, as it can be in front of them and inflict Melee Weakness right before they hit, doubling their damage to the target. It can also pair well with partners with Slay abilities such as Hornbreaker Prince's Reaper upgrade line or Branded Warrior, as it makes frontline enemies easier to kill, though the latter needs some investment in its initial Attack for this to work well.

Its initial Armor also makes Battering Ram much more draftable, allowing that spell to do significant damage from the start of the fight.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
| Heartstone and Largestone are effective on  as with its Armor and Strike ability, it usually should be deployed in front of other units, making its Health valuable.
|-
|
| While ' does not have a way to scale its Attack, it is worth noting that if your deck can scale s Attack (e.g. with Branded Warrior), giving it Multistrike will triple its damage thanks to the Melee Weakness applied. (Unfortunately, the second hit will "use up" the Melee Weakness from the first strike, so you cannot use this to triple the attack of the unit behind '.)
|}
    1.  Pacts & Unit Essence (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)

' generally wants to have units fused into it that increase its bulk to handle the increased damage that is thrown at it with Pact Shards in play. As such, it is good to fuse it with a copy of itself (perhaps using a Hellvent) or other units that give Armor/Health.

Fusing a unit that has a damage scaling mechanism into it such as Alpha Fiend or Branded Warrior could be an idea if ' has Multistrike since Multistrike triples its damage, but you are usually better served doing that to the unit behind '.

Fusing it into another unit that needs bulk is also a commonly good option, as there are many Hellhorned units that appreciate the initial bulk. Perhaps the most notable receipient is Apex Imp, as if you fuse ' into Apex Imp, it will start with 30 Rage as well as Armor.

## Razorsharp Edge
' is an Awoken  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
Dried out vines can deal more deadly blows, but they risk shattering more easily than their hydrated counterparts. My efforts at strengthening these embrittled appendages have proven fruitless.

    1.  Strategy
Razorsharp Edge is Awoken's fastest standalone offensive scaling option, injecting Attack immediately into a unit of your choice. Awoken has no shortage of strong backline DPS units, such as Shattered Shell, Husk Hermit, and Animus of Will, that are able to amplify this Attack immediately into floor-clearing and tank-shredding power. Cross-clan, any unit with Multistrike or Sweep can take advantage of the fast scaling of Razorsharp Edge to the fullest.

The challenge with this card is to manage your Health on that DPS unit, as Health can add up quickly. Even if you do not kill your unit outright with excessive plays, you may inadvertently put yourself under certain thresholds for enemies with Spikes or Sweep themselves. It may be helpful to take Health upgrades at Merchants of Steel to enable you to play Razorsharp Edge multiple times on that unit.

Luckily, Awoken has several ways to mitigate this - most notably, its in-clan Cultivate lines (such as Cultivating Sentient or Awoken Hollow) turn the Health reduction into an advantage. Since Cultivate triggers on the unit with the lowest HP on a floor, Razorsharp Edge reducing your backline's HP will allow the frontline Cultivate tank to continue offensively improving it instead of starting to buff itself. If you are not on a Cultivate line, drafting a Steel Enhancer or finding alternate defensive solutions such as stacking Armor can also offset the HP loss.

Razorsharp Edge can be cast on both friendly and enemy units, and its reduction applies to both current and maximum Health. If cast on a unit (friendly or enemy) with Health or lower, it will kill that unit (the Attack is still applied). This allows it to serve dual-duty in the early-game, allowing you to access backlines with Health or less. A friendly unit killed this way will have its maximum Health reset to 1.

## Rector Flicker
' is the Original Champion  of the Melting Remnant .

His Starter Card is Dreg.

    1.  Lore

Both priest and gang leader, Rector Flicker has taken advantage of the Armageddon at hand and played the Reformed and the Wickless off each other, creating an endless cycle of profit through deceit. I don't agree much with his methods, but his charm is hard to ignore.

    1.  Battle Quotes

- Reformed or Wickless, it's all the same.
- I hope you like wax!
- I could use a guttering right now....
- I can sympathize with the Traitor in some regard...
- Do not presume to know our ways.
- Fancy a light?
- Hell will not be kind to you!
- You haven't even seen the worst of it.
- This wax will burn!
- Say your prayers!
- Even I won't save you now.
- I'd much prefer to be off this train.
- Did you really need to call me into this?
- The lack of wax here is irritating.
- Blessed are the Waxen.
- Is this flame not holy enough for you?
- Blessed are the Wickless.
- Deception can be a great unifier, no?

    1.  Strategy
All three upgrade paths for Rector Flicker require specific strategies to take full advantage of their strengths while covering their weaknesses.

    1.  Burn Bright
The highest priority with Burn Bright is keeping Flicker from burning out while allowing his incredible stats to win fights.

Burn Bright is the most risky of the three choices, especially as a first upgrade. It makes Flicker powerful enough to take on almost anything you'll encounter early on, but unless you gain certain extra starter cards you won't have any way to keep him alive long enough to fight the boss, nor reform him. On your first battle, it may be wise to forgo the extra challenge and defeat the early waves without placing Flicker in your first hand, as you should draw him again in time for the boss. Prioritize gaining cards like Lady of the Reformed, Hallowed Drippings, and Molded which either apply additional stacks of Burnout or allow you to Reform. If you only have a few cards that can reform, try to get a Freezestone for one of them so that it can be saved for immediate use when Flicker burns out.

## Remnant Host
' is a Melting Remnant  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
Tombs are usually formed as a consequence of some horrible accident when guttering. If a Waxer happens to be caught in the wax drip of another, they can be trapped inside until their host melts away.

    1.  Strategy
Remnant Host stands out, even among Melting Remnant units, in its ability to rapidly generate Harvest and Extinguish triggers. When the Remnant Host dies, it spawns two Cardless Draffs that have Burnout 1, meaning they will also die after attacking. This is three Harvest triggers on the same 1 Capacity unit. In addition to this synergy with Harvest units, it becomes even more powerful when combined with the Exploding Candle, Memorial Fund, Mold Braces, or Waxer Snuffer relics. Be aware, however, that this strength can work against the player when facing enemies with Harvest triggers!

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Immortalstone is effective on units who have a low Ember cost and occupy only one Capacity (especially units with Summon or Extinguish affixes). The unit can prevent three hits to your front line unit since it spawns two Draffs, while also letting you benefit from its Extinguish ability multiple times throughout the course of the battle.
|}

    1.  Additional Information
The Draffs summoned by Remnant Host appear directly in front of the Remnant Host's previous position on the floor (or its current position, if its effect was triggered by Intent on Death.) They are also Cardless, and as such cannot be Reformed after death.

## Remnant Pact
' is a Melting Remnant  Card and a Tome in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
Despite their age-long feud, the Wickless and Reformed were able to come to an armistice when the Covenant was formed. That doesn't mean that the rules put in place for their peace were easy to follow.

    1.  Strategy
Remnant Pact is a situational yet powerful and impactful pickup. Endless is an incredibly powerful upgrade for a variety of units that have Summon or Extinguish triggers that you may want to activate many times throughout a combat, such as any Imp or Tomb, or are otherwise low-maintenance, high-impact units that are best when they can be replayed frequently, such as Titan Sentry. With this card, so long as you draw it before that unit dies (or you can Reform that unit and manage Burnout on it), you will be able to reap the full benefits of their abilities over and over again.

While Endless is common as an infusion within the Melting Remnant clan, and is also accessible as a major upgrade in Merchants of Steel, those will not always be accessible when you need them. Remnant Pact offers an alternate way to connect Endless when you have not seen those options yet; this, at the very least, allows your strategy to function until you find a "real" Endless source, or at its best can solve the issue altogether.

This card is particularly strong with Champions that benefit from Endless. As you cannot normally apply that upgrade to most Champions except through Immortality Potion in the DLC, many of their paths were built without the possibility of Endless in mind, making them hyper-efficient if you can connect Remnant Pact reliably with them. Some notable examples include:
- Bristling Sentient, who can be replayed Titan Sentry-style to kill backlines throughout an entire combat;
- Strangler + Predator Wyldenten, who can eternally lock down a floor and come back immediately after being overwhelmed;
- Primordium, who can be replayed after being eaten to continue giving stats;
- Fire Light III Little Fade, who will gain all of the benefits of an Eternal Flame pivot without sacrificing scaling speed.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
|Remnant Pact is at its best when used on Champions that normally wouldn't have access to Endless from a Merchant of Steel. Since you are guaranteed to draw your Champion on turn 1, the Intrinsic from Seekstone will allow you to consistently connect the two cards from the start of combat.
|-
| imageonly=y
|Putting a Freezestone on Remnant Pact allows you to almost always connect it with the right unit, particularly non-Banner Units that do not have draw priority. This hedges against the possibility of drawing Remnant Pact first or on a turn where you can't play it due to Ember restrictions.
|}

## Resin Removal
' is a Melting Remnant  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
When wax hardens into resin, it is possible to chip away the crystallized exterior to expose the soft wax underneath. The process does not come without risks, though.
    1.  Strategy
Resin Removal is a card whose value fluctuates wildly depending on how you play the game, with the trend being that the harder the game becomes, the better Resin Removal becomes in turn. Without the DLC, it is a relatively weak card as most enemies do not naturally have buffs, and some of them that do can be dealt with in various other ways, such as playing on top floor for enemy Rage and Stealth to wear off. This makes it mostly a situational pick into boss fights that forcibly apply buffs to enemy units or debuff your units, such as Rage Fel, Seraph the Temperant, and Seraph the Patient. Resin Removal also completely shuts down Crystalcloak as well as Ring 5's Hidden Assault, but it remains a relatively situational card.

With the DLC, Resin Removal gains much more utility as enemies and bosses can be enhanced with Pact Shards, some of which spawn with status effects such as Spikes, Spell Shield, Damage Shield, or extra Stealth, which can interfere with your normal strategy. This gives it many more turns where it can connect with relevant enemies to neuter otherwise dangerous threats. In the Divinity combat, almost every wave features an upgraded enemy with some sort of intrusive buff, which allows Resin Removal to fully shine.

At high Pact Shard counts, due to the huge density of upgraded enemy waves, Resin Removal becomes a candidate for one of the best cards in the game. It notably completely shuts down the most dangerous aspect of a Pact Shard-upgraded Seraph the Diligent, which is the high amount of Stealth that he spawns in with. This can pose a serious problem for many strategies in Relentless in an even greater capacity than Crystalcloak, and usually makes Diligent the most difficult Seraph variant at high Shards; Resin Removal being able to completely negate that aspect of the fight makes it a premium pick in such runs.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
|Freezestone is a valuable upgrade for Resin Removal, allowing you to hold the card for exactly when you need it, often for the most dangerous upgraded wave (or minor ring Boss) against your strategy.
|-
| imageonly=y
|Emberstone makes Resin Removal free, allowing you to always play it when you see it. At high Pact Shard counts in the DLC, Resin Removal will often always find a target, giving this upgrade more value.
|}

    1.  History

## Restore
' is an Awoken  Card in the Base  Starter Deck. You begin with 3-5 of them depending on your Covenant Rank.

    1.  Lore
One of the many strengths of the Channelers of the Wildwood is their ability to heal their fellow Awoken. Upon our completion of the Rail, they agreed to extend this practice to other Hellborne in need.

    1.  Strategy
' is a rather weak card in general - 2 health per card will not suffice for very long. However, it can be used strategically to allow a unit to barely survive a hit that would kill it.

The main use ' has is to trigger Rejuvenate abilities - and it will often do so twice, since it also provides Regen. If you have this card, it is therefore often worth picking up units with Rejuvenate abilities.

You will typically want it removed by lategame, or at least upgraded with a Surgestone to allow one decent, cheap heal once per fight.

On Regen heavy builds, especially with The Sentient,  can actually be very potent and it could even be good to duplicate 0 cost  over other cards.

## Retch
' is an Umbra  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore

Even the Shroud knows little restraint, sometimes consuming Morsels when, as far as I can tell, no hunger remains. This compulsion, unfortunately, can lead to… ”involuntary expulsion.”

    1.  Strategy
When played, Retch returns all units from the player's eaten pool to their hand. If this would cause the player's hand to exceed 10 cards, excess cards are shuffled into the player's deck.

Retch does not return units from the dead/consumed pool, only the eaten one. Players who wish to use Retch should consider sacrificing unwanted Morsels to block attacks, rather than allowing them to be eaten, so that Retch will retrieve only high-quality Morsels.

Retch allows the player to replay a fully consumed Primordium, making it a very powerful card in many Exiled Umbra builds.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|If your scaling plan revolves around Morsel consumption, keeping ' in play for as long as possible can be quite helpful. Keepstone is good for this (and is especially worth considering if your main attacker is Morsel-Made or otherwise infused with it), but Freezestone also works if you are playing Primordium.
|}

## Ritual of Battle
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
I have learned that the Hellhorned can gain temporary strength through self-infliction of pain. I find the ritual savage, but it proves to be even more effective when performed over the remains of their opponents.

    1.  Strategy
Ritual of Battle is one of the Hellhorned's fundamental offensive scaling options. 10 Rage is an incredible amount of power in one card play, being the equivalent of Attack; if you have the means to cycle into this card and play it multiple times, your offense can often be solved from the very start of the run. While its Ember cost may be intimidating, its effect is so powerful that it's often worth pathing to an early Merchant of Magic immediately to reduce its cost with two size=20pxs, making it much more playable and a valuable duplication target at Hellvents and the Fissure event. Overall, Ritual of Battle is a strong, impactful Common draft well worth investing into, and should be strongly considered for any run.

Ritual of Battle often competes with Fledgling Imp as a Rage scaling option. If the two are offered together in a Common pack, the correct choice can often depend on your starting Artifacts and node generation.
- If your first few Merchants of Magic are located on strong sides of a ring, or if you suspect that your damage will come from one dedicated DPS unit with Sweep or several Multistrike stacks (most commonly with the Awoken clan), Ritual of Battle will often be superior to focus all of the Rage onto one unit without needing Capacity to play.
:*Ritual of Battle is also superior for Incant strategies from the Stygian Guard.
- If you have Imp-supporting relics or Imperialist Shardtail Queen as your Champion or have multiple strong Merchants of Steel in your first few rings to hunt for an size=20px upgrade, Fledgling Imp will often be better.
:*Fledgling Imp is also superior if you are able to set up a dense floor with multiple units and have enough Capacity to play it to scale that entire floor.

It is also not uncommon to have one in your starting cards and take the other if your line can support both, diversifying your shops and speeding up your scaling to incredible degrees.

## Sacrificial Resurrection
' is a Melting Remnant  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
When new wax is unavailable to reform a fallen Waxer, the more selfless Reformed choose to donate a portion of their own form to the resurrection.

    1.  Strategy
As  consumes a lot of cards in the deck, while leaving the player to control which cards are consumed, it is a card that can prepare an Infinite.

Sacrificial Resurrection consumes all other cards in the player's hand, regardless of their type. It can therefore be used as an efficient way to remove Scourges and temporarily remove Blights, including those that cannot normally be consumed or purged. Since cards in the consumed pile still count as part of the player's deck, effects that check the number of Blights in the player's deck, such as the summon trigger of Dante the Deceptive, will still count cards consumed by Sacrificial Resurrection.

As consumed cards and dead units share a pile, unit cards consumed with Sacrificial Resurrection can be reformed.

## Shadoweater
' is a Banner Unit of the Umbra .

    1.  Lore

A piece of the Umbra Shroud, blindly searching for morsels and metals to collect, returning them to the collective Shroud consciousness. Though when I say collect, I really mean eat them whole.

    1.  Strategy
' is best placed on the 1st floor to optimize its Gorge effect to get rid of backline enemies early and heal back any damage taken between turns. Since it needs a steady supply of Morsels to sustain, you will need good, consistent Morsel generation and enough Capacity to make the most out of this card. Additional Ember generators are recommended due to its somewhat high Ember cost.

Since ' needs to eat Morsels constantly, it's hard to place another unit on the same floor as the '. Morselmaker works well with it, allowing you get two Gorge procs consistently while also adding a bit of frontline damage. With an additional Capacity, you can place up to four Morsels per turn.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

! Description
|-
|
| Thornstone is effective on front line units (which typically have a high default health value), because the damage it deals is typically dealt multiple times as each enemy on the floor attacks the front line unit.
|}

Morsel-Made is very synergistic unit to fuse with ', allowing you to not have to worry about Capacity for Morsels and killing backline units before they can attack, making it able to survive much longer.

## Shadowsiege
Shadowsiege is a Banner Unit of the Umbra .

    1.  Lore
I was told that the first Constructs were made of discarded stone and a coordinated Antumbra effort. It is considered a prestigious position among Morsels. These were able to speed up the excavation of the Crucible tenfold.

    1.  Strategy
Shadowsiege immediately stands out from any other unit in the game - it has enough Attack to kill any non-boss, non-DLC enemy, and is capable of winning runs as the focal point of the run if supported.

Unfortunately, it also stands out as perhaps the most difficult unit to play, costing Ember and Capacity to play, which is Ember higher and Capacity more than what you are able to play with no Artifacts on turn 1. Remember that it is a Banner Unit, so you are guaranteed to draw it early.

Unfortunately, because units are drafted in Rings 1-4, you usually need to draft Shadowsiege before you will be able to play it, and while the Capacity requirement can be fulfilled by taking a Light of Seraph, the Ember requirement requires specific enablers that you may or may not find. As such, taking Shadowsiege is almost always a risk, though it is more likely to pay off if you have a way to gain Ember such as Perils of Production or reduce the cost of units such as Forever Flame. It also requires a fairly strong deck, as it will be a dead draw until you can play it. In addition, if you are using cards such as Perils of Production or Space Prism to help play Shadowsiege, you will need to have a plan to handle the first few turns of fights before you can play Shadowsiege as you may not draw back around to it in time.

Once you have figured out how to play Shadowsiege, upgrading it with size=20px and using spells such as Furnace Tap, Immortal Trade, and Umbra Stone on it will often be able to win the run. Using Floor Overstacking to give it allies will tremendously help as well.

Alternatively, you can force Shadowsiege out using Sketches of Salvation to summon it onto the middle floor. You will most likely need to remove a few units early to ensure Sketches targets it, however this strategy can be incredibly powerful once you've removed other units and duplicated a couple Shadowsiege copies- especially once you upgrade it with size=20px and/or size=20px, you can win entire combats (barring Haste enemies) without ever playing a single card!

    1.  Strategy

    1.  Royalty
Difficult to use on its own but scales with Tomb, Imp, or Burnout strategies. Rally triggers activate when summoning additional (non-Morsel) units.  To center a Royalty run off disposable Imps, it requires both the means of generating more imps to place (such as Imp Parade or Endless) and to get rid of them efficiently (such as Imperialist or other Sacrifice cards), as neither aspect can reliably allow enough Rally triggers without support.

    1.  Imperialist
A strong, if risky, direction to take the Shardtail Queen. So long as you do not run out of Imps, and are able to protect them until she can use her action, she can devastate entire waves of foes at a time. However, this requires her to be on the lower levels, and usually to avoid putting the Imps in the front row in order to help them survive enemy attacks (though making her Quick, such as by using a Wildwood Tome for instance, makes her immediately trigger her action). Furthermore, it requires a very significant number of Imps in the deck to be activated reliably, though taking a rank or two in Imp Parade can help alleviate this.

## Sharpen
' is an Awoken  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
I've found discarded Hollow armor serves as a perfect tool to sharpen dull weapons.

    1.  Strategy
Sharpen is one of the least efficient cards in the game and is inarguably the Awoken clan's worst Common card at high Covenant Ranks due to two major fundamental flaws that hold it back significantly.

The first is that the combination of buffs it confers upon units is completely paradoxical - you generally want Attack buffs on a DPS unit, but you would want to put Spikes on a frontline tank, and it is rare that these two will be the same unit. The second is that the buffs it gives are simply not worth spending Ember on in the first place. Compared to Steel Enhancer, which gives only 1 less Attack but also Health at Ember, or Razorsharp Edge which pumps Attack into a unit, Sharpen falls far behind. This is made even worse by the aforementioned paradoxical nature of giving Attack and Spikes at the same time - not only is the raw value of each buff inefficient for the energy spent, but you will more than likely be wasting half of the benefits on a unit that can't use it.

While Sharpen can clear backlines well in the early-game, it falls off heavily without investment into the mid- and late-game, often being extremely difficult to play while also getting everything else in the combat sorted. This is especially true in the DLC, where backlines can be buffed to have more health if you take Pact Shards, meaning Sharpen will fail to do its job entirely. Thus, Sharpen can often even be a removal priority if it shows up in your opener, unless you have a Split Anvil to weaponize its high cost, Gnarled Root to make the Attack buff more efficient, or have no other methods of scaling.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"

| imageonly=y imageonly=y

It is worth noting that Sharpen is often not a good use of the PactShards needed for size=20px unless you need to take more PactShards to meet the Divinity requirement.
|-
| imageonly=y
|As one of the least efficient cards in the game, Sharpen is often a priority for removal; purging it with Purgestone can be a decent use of PactShards.
|-

|}
