## Shattered Shell
' is a Banner Unit of the Awoken .

    1.  Lore
When Husk Hermits grow too large for their already restrictive shell, they keep the shattered steel pieces for offensive use. Against my wishes, the Channelers have hoarded Pyre shards to further enhance the power of these creatures.

    1.  Strategy
Shattered Shell is the more aggressive of the two Awoken Sweep units, having been designed as a pure damage unit from the start, unlike Husk Hermit. While it starts off with lower base stats than Husk Hermit, it has a scaling effect on Slay to gain more Attack, potentially allowing it to snowball in strength. Sweep is an incredibly powerful keyword in Monster Train - just by having the effect, Shattered Shell innately takes buffs and force multipliers very well. With any source of Multistrike, or Trample applied to it, it can shred even the densest waves of enemies by itself.

Shattered Shells middling Health is a double-edged sword. On one hand, it allows Shattered Shell to immediately be able to take Cultivate triggers from a frontline Awoken Hollow or Cultivating Sentient, without fear of them beginning to Cultivate themselves and take scaling away from the backline. On the other, it significantly impairs the survivability of Shattered Shell compared to Husk Hermit - it is vulnerable to Spikes and Sweep enemies, particularly full Spikes waves where it will quickly kill itself taking down the enemies on each floor. It is also flimsy into Relentless if the boss manages to kill your tank or has some way to access your backline.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
| Multistrike on a Sweep unit is critical - it is possibly one of the most damage-efficient upgrades you can make, provided you have ample sources of Attack buffs in your strategy. As long as your strategy can scale fast enough to defeat or heavily damage the first wave with tank enemies, this will single-handedly solve your wave-clearing abilities for the run.
|-
| imageonly=y
| Sweep units also synergize well with Quick, providing a massive amount of survivability against waves as Shattered Shell will now kill dangerous backline enemies before they can attack into your floor.
|-
| imageonly=y imageonly=y imageonly=y
| Health upgrades prop up the meager Health of Shattered Shell, protecting it against enemies with Spikes or Sweep. Heartstone provides a significant boost in health that stays relevant for the whole run, while Battlestone and Largestone can help raise the unit's initial damage thresholds, allowing it to begin getting Slay triggers earlier. Keep in mind that you should generally not take Largestone if your frontline tank is Capacity, unless you already have a space solution in the run.
|}

## Shroud Mitosis
' is an Umbra  Card in Monster Train. It is a spell in the Umbra clan.
    1.  Lore

I thought that only the Umbra could split themselves off into more Morsels, but it seems that the Antumbra can create even more copies of themselves. I guess that’s why there’s a saying about a Morsel in a dark mine...

    1.  Strategy
This card is most effectively used with Making of a Morsel to generate multiple Morsel Miners efficiently. You should first place Morsels up to Capacity and then use Shroud Mitosis for maximum effect. Note that the maximum number of units on a floor is 7.

With a consistent source of morsels every turn - such as Morselmaker or Abandoned Antumbra - Shroud Mitosis with a Keepstone can also be used effectively to generate more morsels each turn for a unit with a Gorge ability. Note that consistently generating morsels every turn is key for executing this strategy well, as Shroud Mitosis can only be played (and therefore retained for the next turn with Holdover) if at least one morsel is present.

## Sinner's Burden
' is a Clanless  Scourge Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
A contraption designed by Seraph himself to punish the sinful. It unlocks when the wearer is deemed penitent enough, a state that has never truly been defined and left to the judgment of the punisher. As expected, this has been used as a tool of oppression against us in the invasion.

    1.  Strategy
When faced with enemies that give Scourge cards, Emberdrain can be dangerous as well as cost randomization by Volatile Gauge. Setting up on the bottom floor can prevent too many Scourges, as can targeted damage or AoE spells. Sinner's Salve will make it cost Ember.

Note that Sinner's Burden, unlike other Scourge cards added by enemies, does not have a penalty for keeping it in hand. The only penalty is wasting a draw on it. Therefore, if you do not expect to draw it again (because the battle will end first), there is no benefit to playing it.

For the First time, I've spoken with these dream beings! Solgard, the priest of the Stygian's Titan, spoke of a mass Exodus of martyrs. Martyrs who gave up their souls for the sake of Heaven and Hell. But why does no one living know of this great sacrifice? These answers only create more questions...

Solgard gains one stack of Shard per Incant, and these Shards power up the rest of his abilities. Thus, Solgard is at his best in a deck that can play many spells in a single turn, and encourages the player to lean into the Incant lines that Stygian Guard provides. Most of his paths are vulnerable in the early game due to their low Health and ramp-up time, but also due to how it will take time to set up a good Incant deck. However, once you are able to get past the initial hurdles, he becomes a very effective cleanup and boss killer unit.

## Soul Cripple

The Wurmkin claim to be a clan of knowledge and peace while they hoard the lore of Titans as the world around them dies. However, no amount of clever words can write over the violent truth on which they live: Echoes need Souls. For a clan so obsessed with its own secrecy, those who will volunteer their souls are hard to come by. With so many Echoes breathing, the number of souls required to keep them alive is... unsettling. But I can no longer judge on these scales — that time has passed.

## Spike of the Hellhorned
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train and a Railspike.

    1.  Lore
In an act of unity unheard of in Hellhorned culture, Grrrg the Railbeater snapped his own horn to seal the Rail in their Ring, bringing the Hellhorned into our Covenant. Grrrg the Railbeater was thereafter known as Grrrg Onehorn.

    1.  Strategy
Spike of the Hellhorned is not only arguably the worst Railspike in the game, but one of the worst cards in Hellhorned altogether. It gives 2 Rage and Armor per Ember, which is even less efficient than Enflame (which is already a very inefficient card for its cost). Because the Rage and Armor gain scales only linearly with Ember spent, this Spike does not take size=20px upgrades as well as the others, as it is already an inefficient card and reductions will not directly change its efficiency.

Spike of the Hellhorned is only worth considering if you have synergies for it, such as Impish Scholar to bring it back and reuse it with an excess of Ember, The Unbroken Horn to store Ember for a big play, or The First Hellpact to make this card give a baseline 6 Rage and Armor. Even with all of those synergies in mind, Spike of the Hellhorned is among the worst users of them - many other X Cost-cost cards or consumable cards become broken if such synergies are present, while Spike of the Hellhorned merely becomes functional.

Overall, this card is usually a desperation pick at best, and while it can provide emergency damage scaling should your run lack any other methods of generating Attack buffs, it should often be avoided over other Rare cards.

## Spike of the Stygian
' is a Stygian Guard  Card and a Railspike in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore

The Stygian Guard was formed with our Rail of the Covenant, created by Tethys to protect the sealed Unnamed Titan. The Guard was wary of our Covenant from the beginning, worried that it would make the Pyre vulnerable to outside attacks. Unfortunately, they were correct.

    1.  Strategy

' usually struggles to have much of an impact on its own - even if Ember is spent on it, 3 Sap and 15 Frostbite is not the strongest effect you can get for that cost. ' is at its best as a win condition against Relentless bosses for decks that are able to generate moreEmber. Sap becomes more and more powerful the more stacks you apply, eventually giving your units an entire extra turn against a boss per stack, and Frostbite also gets stronger with each additional stack.

However, the Stygian Guard does not have spells or units that increase your Ember. As such, ' is dependent on cross-clan synergies and Artifacts to be able to generate a large amount of Ember. It's not a commonly good card, but it can be really strong in some circumstances.

' plays particularly poorly into The Last Divinity, which cleanses all debuffs with Purify every turn, and Seraph the Chaste which also gains Purify at 130 Pact Shards and if you are at/above Covenant 10.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Stackstone is almost always positive for Spike of the Stygian, only applying less Sap and Frostbite if it is played for only 12=Ember, being neutral for 22=Ember, and positive for anything above that. Especially if you are able to generate large amounts of2=Ember (which you should be able to if you're drafting Spike of the Stygian anyways), it can almost double the amount of Sap and Frostbite applied.
|-
|
|Freezestone is a strong upgrade for ' in the right deck - as mentioned before, this card is at its best when used against bosses, and Freezestone lets you draw it only once and always have it at the right time (though if you have random discard cards like Forgone Power that you play, this is less useful).
|-
|
|Emberstone is a solid upgrade for ', adding 1 more Sap and 5 more Frostbite.
|}

## Spikedriver Colony
' is a Clanless  Card in Monster Train.

Obtained from the Heph the Blacksmith Concealed Caverns event.

After getting 4 or more copies of the card in your deck, Heph comes back to give them Attack and removes the Extinguish.

    1.  Lore
I knew I couldn't rely on anyone to build the rail for me, so I set off to do it myself, creating automatic creatures to drive rail spikes into the ground and lay the track that would later be broken.

    1.  Strategy

Make sure to upgrade them before playing them as any purchased upgrades from the shop are copied when it's extinguished. Let them die on purpose. As they are Capacity units with Multistrike, it can be good to upgrade their initial Attack value and putting them behind a tank or distribute them among several floors.

' pairs well with Little Fade's Fire Light upgrade path, as their Capacity allows several of them to fit on the same floor as her.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Speedstone and Frenzystone are both quite effective on ', allowing it to attack first, or further increasing its total amount of hits on the floor. It is recommended to pair one of these with a Health upgrade, as it has poor survivability otherwise.
|-
|
|Heartstone patches up s poor Health and allows it to survive longer, without increasing its Capacity.
|-
|
|When using ' as blockers or in a reform deck, Immortalstone and Wickstone can be good upgrades.
|-
|
|Largestone is effective on '. This way they duplicate into four Capacity , Attack , Health. With an additional upgrade this is great for their own floor or as medium supports with other units. Note that this might make it harder to kill them initially.
|-
|
|As ' has Multistrike, extra attack is particularly useful on them.
|}

    1.  Suggested Unit Essences (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)
In general, ' prefers infusions that either increase its stats, or give it beneficial Status Effects, without increasing its Capacity.

Paraffin Enforcer is an excellent infusion for ' if you are playing the Melting Remnant  clan. With several of them attacking at once on the same floor, their Attack will skyrocket thanks to all of the quickly accumulating Rage. When paired with Quick or a source of Dazed, Sap, or Stealth, and proper Burnout management, they become nigh-unstoppable.

Lady of the House is another good infusion from the same clan, giving ' more immediate power and survivability, and allowing you to lean into other strategies involving Burnout, including opening up Hallowed Halls as a scaling option.

Notable infusions from other clans include Husk Hermit for its Health, Steelworker for rapidly stacking Armor, and Morselmaker for its passive Attack/Health scaling.

## Spine Chief
' is the Original Champion  of the Wurmkin .

His Starter Card is Fracture.

    1.  Lore
For all of their obsession with knowledge and Titanlore, the Wurmkin are focused to a fault, worrying only about the study in front of them. As such, they’ve appointed a Chief as both protector and leader. This Chief’s experience with combat is limited, but his wits must be sharp to have kept the clan hidden for so long. Thankfully, Wurmkin claws are as deadly as they are functional, so should the Chief ever really need to fight it won’t be totally unprepared.

    1.  Strategy

    1.  The Decayer
You can use The Decayer Spine Chief as a tank in front of your carry unit, or place it in its own floor to clear Backline units. As reap benefits on Multiple floors, you may wants to set up on a lower floor and fill all floors with Charged Echoes if possible. With enough sustain and echoes, Decayer Spine Chief can kill most units throughout the level, leaving you to scale freely on top floor for relentless.

    1.  The Corruptor
Put as many units as you can on the floor with The Corruptor Spine Chief. Note that the maximum number of friendly units on a floor is 7. Multistrike units benefit greatly, so do 1 space units or the ability to ascend and descend units onto the floor. You can increase your charged echoes for extra damage and the buff will stay during relentless. Some interesting builds include adding Morsels onto your floor to attack, using Imps or other 1 capacity units with Multistrike and stacking several eggs behind Corruptor (not that you will need to hatch the eggs very quickly)

    1.  The Infector
The Infector Spine Chief can act as either a carry or tank due to its high health. Use it with Inspire units, hatch eggs, or Extract spells. Inspire will trigger multiple times per turn.

## Spreading Spores
' is an Awoken  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
When thorns find their mark, they release spores into the air to incapacitate unsuspecting victims. These poor souls soon transform into Awoken and feed the vines that first spread the spores. On the embarrassingly frequent occasions that I'm stung by these thorns, I remove them before any real damage occurs.

    1.  Strategy

Spreading Spores is a good counter against the Consume effect from Seraph the Diligent

## Steelworker
' is a Banner Unit of the Hellhorned .

    1.  Lore
Along with a team of imps, these demons are responsible for the creation of all Hellhorned armors and weapons. While not usually on the front lines, their centuries of working metal gives them a comfortable tendency towards combat.

    1.  Strategy

' applies Armor to all of your units every turn, building up the defensive strength of the floor over time and fortifying backline units against Sweep and Spikes that enemies may have.

As with all units that apply a benefit to all units on a floor, ' is at its best when floor overstacking is used - the more units occupying its floor, the more potent its Resolve effect is.

Apex Imp is especially able to take advantage of the Armor provided by also getting Rage as well as armor when benefitting from this effect.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Heartstone and Largestone are effective on ' as it is often as good a choice as any to be on the front line of a floor, since it isn't particularly offensively oriented itself. Largestone can interfere with your ability to crowd s floor, so consider whether your deck can handle this.
|}

    1.  Pacts & Unit Essence (The Last Divinity (DLC) only)

' is usually drafted for its Resolve effect, and since it is preserved in full when its essence is fused into another unit, it is usually fused into a different unit rather than the other way around.

Occasionally, ' is simply your bulkiest unit, and you need it to be taking hits. In that case, units that provide additional bulk can be good units to fuse into '.

## Sting
' is an Awoken  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
Some trees drop acorns to seed new life - the Awoken shoot thorns. To protect myself, I wore thick leather armor on my first visit to the Wildwood. I can't say it helped much...

    1.  Strategy
Sting is a "cantrip"-style card - it has a moderately useful effect for the early-game, softening up early tanks with its 10 damage, then replaces itself on the next turn's draw. It tends to be a decent starting card and a net neutral draft if the rest of a Common Awoken card reward pack is not needed for your run. With synergies from Artifacts such as Channelheart and Thorn Casing, Sting can stay relevant throughout an entire run, although it does require multiples to function as a major part of your damage plan.

If leaning into Stings, it is generally recommended to use a generator such as Vinemother or Thornfruit instead of drafting them directly from the two Common Awoken card packs. Note that this may not always be the best line, as having a high quantity of Stings can dilute your deck, making it more difficult to draw into the higher-impact cards that you would need to answer a wave. This usually synergizes best with Incant strategies that do not care as much about what spells are being played on the floor, minimizing the risk of this deck clog being an issue.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
|You may not always want Sting to cycle around in your deck lategame if you have ample delayed card draw effects already. Surgestone provides a power boost and lets you Consume it out of your deck for free after the first cycle.
|}

## Subsuming Blade
' is a Melting Remnant  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
This blade grows larger with each use, slowly accumulating more wax and becoming more dangerous. Reluctantly, I created it with the Wickless when Hell was invaded to defend against the advancing Winged.

    1.  Strategy
The ideal upgrade set is Holdover + Energy. Holdover allows the damage on Subsuming Blade to scale up reasonably quickly, which obviates the need for any damage upgrades. Reducing the cost from 2 to 1 makes Subsuming Blade much easier to play alongside other cards. Subsuming Blade is a very flexible card, as it can be used for backline removal, removing your own units (e.g. scaling Bounty Stalker via Extinguish trigger + Reform, Imps), or dealing reasonable damage to enemy heavies.

## Tethys Titansbane
' is the Original Champion  of the Stygian Guard .

Her Starter Card is Frozen Lance.

    1.  Lore
Do not be fooled by her harmless demeanor. Tethys was the first to agree to the containment of the Titan for our Covenant's sake. She has since become the most revered member of the Stygian Guard and my most helpful supporter in the defense of Hell.

    1.  Battle Quotes

- The Titan will wake soon.
- We can't keep the Titan asleep forever.

- The Covenant was necessary. Now removing Seraph is.
- Feel the Titan's wrath!
- Yahaha!
- Even if the Covenant is broken we cannot falter in our calling.
- The Stygian know no mercy.
- Unnamed give me power!
- Taste the arcane!
- We must protect the Titan, no matter what.
- Does the Titan still sleep?
- Yaaargh!
- Titan's might!

    1.  Strategy
Tethys, being a Capacity unit with strong effects in each path, is a versatile unit for most of the game that can slot into most strategies, particularly in-clan with the Stygian Guard. However, in The Last Divinity DLC, she becomes much less effective, requiring huge amounts of support to be able to contribute meaningfully against The Last Divinity as well as enemy units enhanced by Pact Shards. This is due to her low Health across all paths, making her particularly vulnerable to Spikes and Sweep upgrades and enemies. Most notoriously, the Divinity's top-floor Sweep auto-kills her on any path at high Covenant Ranks except for Handheld Totem III.

## The Sentient
' is the Original Champion  of the Awoken .

Her Starter Card is Restore.

    1.  Lore
This Hollow was able to sever her connection to the Wildwood, slowly awakening to the prison her soul was trapped in, but still agreeing to champion our Covenant. Now she has repurposed her armor as makeshift shields to keep the rest of the vines at bay, becoming more aggressive with each attempt at recapture.

    1.  Battle Quotes

- No longer trapped...

- I can... feel?
- Awoken at last.

    1.  Strategy
As a frontline tank, The Sentients general role is to soak up damage for backliners; oftentimes this means that you'll want to pair it with frail, high-damage units such as Horned Warrior, Draff, Bog Fly and Spikedriver Colony as well as the in-clan Animus of Will and Animus of Speed. Note that the Sentient pairs poorly with Umbra strategies, as Morsels are, by default, eaten by the frontmost non-Morsel unit which will be The Sentient in most cases if it's being used as a tank, which is oftentimes not ideal as you'll want Morsels to be eaten by specialized Umbra units with Gorge triggers/some other unit that scales better off Morsels instead, though Morsel-Made solves this problem handily.

    1.  Explosive

As with all of s upgrade lines, the Explosive line serves as a tank. This line in particular specializes in heavily denting bulky frontline enemies by turning each healing spell into a potent attack.

It requires you to aggressively draft healing cards and invest in card draw to use it to the fullest - note that if you only heal ' once per turn, the Explosive line only ends up dealing a bit more than the Spikes line would to just the front enemy. This makes Edge Prior an especially good partner for this line. The starting Restore cards are also quite decent with this line, often letting you use the ability twice - once on heal, and once on the Regen heal after combat.

## The Ultimate Penance
' is a Clanless  Scourge Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
The Winged believe in the total constriction of the body to repent for mortal sins. Once the restraints have been applied, they are rarely removed until the sinners' life has left them. Seraph has wanted me encased in one of these for quite some time now, but I've been able to keep out of reach - for now.

    1.  Strategy
When faced with enemies that give scourge cards, Emberdrain can be dangerous as well as cost randomization by Volatile Gauge. Setting up on the bottom floor can prevent too many Scourges and so can targeted damage spells or AOE spells. Sinner's Salve can make it cost Ember.

## Thorned Hollow
' is a Banner Unit of the Awoken .

    1.  Lore
There is a clearing in the Wildwood where the vines turn brittle and become covered in thorns. While impossible for me to visit, I've heard that the Hollows that root there are afflicted with a similar transformation.

    1.  Strategy
Thorned Hollow is a strong tank that can singlehandedly carry your run's early-game by itself, while retaining value later on in the run and potentially even becoming your main line. It gains Spikes 3 on a Rejuvenate, meaning that it weaponizes Primary Awoken's Restores extremely well, allowing it to kill all backline enemies in the early rings after just a few triggers while severely denting tanks in the front line as well. With its high 75 maximum Health, it is also incredibly sturdy into Relentless; if you have high enough Regen stacks, it will even continue gaining Spikes in Relentless, further reducing the number of turns you need to defeat the boss.

The main flaws of Thorned Hollow are that it is somewhat slow to scale to address enemies later in the game, and requires that you dedicate resources to scaling its Spikes, taking away those resources from buffing your offensive units that would usually deal with frontline tanks. This poses a problem as Thorned Hollow is not able to defeat tanks on its own, causing a strategy over-reliant on its Spikes to leak frontline enemies into the Pyre room. You can opt to damage scale Thorned Hollow instead and play it dual-duty, but with no innate force multiplier on it, you may still not achieve the numbers necessary.

However, this can quickly change if you see critical Artifacts early that can anchor a full Thorned Hollow run. Gnarled Root and Petrified Crucible are the biggest hits, boosting Attack based on Spikes and doubling Spikes damage, respectively. These speed up the scaling process significantly and address the biggest weaknesses of this unit, allowing it better chances to defeat tanks that it would usually struggle with. Rootsplit Mask is also a fantastic hit, forcing enemies to swing into Thorned Hollow twice on top floor and thus effectively doubling the damage they take on the floor. With any (or several) of these relics in tow, Thorned Hollow can become a very strong late-game plan as well. Be on the lookout for Edge Prior or Shard Channeler as well, which give big powerups to your Thorned Hollow floor by making your healing free and further increasing the power of your Spikes, respectively.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

## Titan Sentry
' is a Banner Unit of the Stygian Guard .

    1.  Lore
Stationed all across the Sea floor, generations of these Guardians have been tasked with the protection of the Unnamed Titan. Their role was entirely ceremonial until now.
    1.  Strategy
Titan Sentry is an excellent multipurpose tank; it is capable of defending units behind it, killing low-Health enemies (including high-Attack and Support-class backlines) on the first floor, and softening up Heavy-class enemies and even bosses by applying high Frostbite stacks for your sturdier scaling units to finish off on higher floors. These traits allow it to slot into nearly every strategy, even ones that don't lean very much into Stygian Guard synergies.

Although it has a solid Health, it isn't an enormous amount, and it needs take hits to activate its Revenge ability. In addition, its ability is most effective on the first floor where enemies suffer the most from the Frostbite it inflicts. This means it will take a beating, and it will struggle to stay alive through most fights without a source of survivability (such as Armor, Regen, or a Health upgrade) or Endless applied to it. Even if this is the case and Titan Sentry has no other defensive support, it can still contribute well by wiping out backline enemies in the first few waves, allowing time for your main line to power up.

However, if Titan Sentry receives Endless, all of its longevity issues will essentially be solved as you will only require Ember every few turns to replay it. Such an upgrade also provides immense security into Relentless, allowing you to replay Titan Sentry on every floor to soften up the ring boss with Frostbite and waste turns to decrement buffs they may have, such as Lifesteal or Stealth. If fully upgraded, Titan Sentry can even be a valuable duplication target at Hellvents to load up floors with crippling amounts of Frostbite twice.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
| Endless is by far the best upgrade that Titan Sentry can receive. It singlehandedly solves the unit's longevity issues, allowing it to wave clear throughout an entire combat, and making it extremely low-maintenance as you no longer have to dedicate other resources to keeping it alive.
|-
| imageonly=y imageonly=y
| Titan Sentry is best used as a wave-clearing unit on lower floors by itself; therefore, Capacity is not usually a concern for it, allowing it to take both Largestone and Major Refraction for very little penalty.
|-
| imageonly=y
| A simple Health is always effective on Titan Sentry; as a common upgrade, it allows you to quickly get Titan Sentry fully upgraded, while still providing a significant boost in survivability.
|}

## Torch
' is a Hellhorned  Card in the Base  Starter Deck. You begin with 3-5 of them depending on your Covenant Rank.

    1.  Lore
The Hellhorned have learned to live with the unpredictability of the Scorched Plains, even going so far as to retain some of the flaming boulders for themselves, despite my attempts to dissuade them.

    1.  Strategy
' is a fairly practical starter card, as it is able to target and kill back line enemies that are otherwise out of reach of your units. This can preserve Unit health, Pyre health, take out a key supporting unit behind the boss, or kill a Collector, among other things.

Note that ' can also be used on your own units, which can be useful - for example, if you need to remove an Imp to make space for a different unit, or trigger a Revenge ability on one of your units.

Be wary of taking a Armor Emblem or Ancient Hate trial with this card, as those will dramatically reduce s usefulness.

Also, ' becomes less useful on Ring 4 and beyond, as there are fewer enemies with Health or less. ' becomes borderline useless for killing enemies past Ring 7 without an upgrade or source of Magic Power.

Unless this card is upgraded or purged prior to Ring 7, or you have another source of Magic Power, such as Tempered Talisman, it becomes borderline deadweight unless you use it as a Revenge Trigger spell, as it becomes unable to kill even the frailest of enemies at that point.

## Transcendimp
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
When a demon dies under the weight of their horns, they are reborn as impish infants, restarting their molting cycle. These imps are quite possibly the most powerful Hellhorned in existence - seeing that all has come before and all that will come after. While this power is lost upon their first molting, they've been crucial in my research of their Ring.

    1.  Strategy

' is one of the few cards that enables Infinites.

While Transcendimp's ability obviously synergizes well with other Imps, it also resolves Summon abilities of non-Imp units, such as Awoken Hollow or Penumbra's Architect path. Any such ability treats Transcendimp as though it had the ability (e.g. copying an Awoken Hollow's summon effect gives Transcendimp maximum health, not the Awoken Hollow, and copying Architect Penumbra's summon effect adds extra2=Capacity to Transcendimp's floor, not Penumbra's floor).

Transcendimp's ability is not itself a Summon ability. Furthermore, Summon effects triggered by playing a Transcendimp do not count as Summon effects. Together, these rules mean that multiple Transcendimps--while potentially very powerful--do not directly amplify each other's effectiveness, and that Ashes of the Fallen will result in each previous Summon ability resolving twice when Transcendimp is played, not four times.

Transcendimp also pairs extremely well with effects that return creatures from the Consume pile, such as the Melting Remnant's Reform mechanic. These can not only be used to repeatedly return Transcendimp but also to repeatedly return units with Summon abilities. Each time such a unit is summoned, they will add their Summon ability to the list for Transcendimp to trigger again. Reform is especially good because a Reformed non-burnout unit will die quickly the first few times it reforms before it builds up to a workable number of Burnout stacks.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Immortalstone is extremely effective on ', letting you recur all previous Summon abilities on each turn (or potentially even more with e.g. Imp-portant Work), which is extremely powerful with even a moderate amount of Summon abilities. You do need to have a way to reliably have ' die, which can be difficult depending on what summon effects have previously occurred. Any card that sacrifices Imps helps, but even Torch or other damage spells that can target your units can do this too.
|-
|
|Wickstone causes ' to die immediately after being played, freeing up space the next turn automatically. Combined with Endless (see Immortalstone), you can play ' every turn and guarantee that it will die and return to your hand every time.
|}

## Vent
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
The Boneshaker is on my own design. Fueled by the last shards of the Pyre, the train is robust and dependable. One of its lesser-known features is the ability to unleash pressurize steam on invaders.

    1.  Strategy
' hits all enemies on a floor, making it a valuable way to clear out weak enemies.

In Rings 1-3, it can kill most backline enemies with Ember, and you usually will be spending that much, as its damage does not scale very well with Ember spent. You should usually play it at the end of the turn when no other spells that cost Ember need to be played.

' should be given a damage upgrade as soon as possible, as it will apply to all enemies - not only does this allow it to keep killing backline enemies later in the game, it also allows it to be effective with a Ember investment (albeit with the constraint that you have to play it after all spells costing Ember).

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Powerstone allows ' to clear all Support-type units in the game (without The Last Divinity (DLC) or Armor Emblem) even when used with 0 Ember.
|-
|
|Extremestone (The Last Divinity (DLC) only) allows ' to clear out all Support-type enemies, even if they are Pact Shard-enhanced.
|-
|
|Keepstone allows ' to be your consistent solution for backline removal, allowing you to devote your other resources towards killing heavy enemies and bosses.
|}

## Vinemother
' is a Banner Unit of the Awoken .

    1.  Lore
These Awoken focus solely on the reproduction of the Wildwood, shedding their steel armor to further spread their thorns with each new surge of life. The Mothers are quite small in stature, but the shell around them provides immense natural protection. Along with the Channelers, they've been indispensable guides during my time in the Wildwood.

    1.  Strategy
At first, it can be tempting to place Vinemother in the backliner to utilize her passive ability, similar to Wildwood Custodian. However, this strategy fails to capitalize on her healthpool, which makes Vinemother a strong frontliner, especially in the earlier fights or when upgraded with something to improve her tankiness, like Heartstone.

Effects to increase the effectiveness of Sting cards, like Channelheart or Thorn Casing, only compound her effectiveness. Other mechanics like Spell Damage and Incant can also help the player get additional value out of Vinemothers free spells – making her strong in combination with a Stygian deck.

When using Vinemother, it is important to remember not to over-saturate your deck with Sting cards, making it difficult to cycle through to more impactful cards. For this reason, it is sometimes correct to intentionally kill or sacrficice Vinemother during a fight.

Vinemother is also a very strong counter to Seraph the Diligent, giving you a free spell each turn to counteract Diligent consuming the first spell you play each turn.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

## Void Binding
' is an Umbra  Card in Monster Train. It is a spell in the Umbra clan.

    1.  Lore

When Void shards were first found, they were considered to be of little value. So, they were collected and dumped to the depths of the Crucible. Later, the Umbra and I found that the shards can mimic the effects of any nearby gems, making them incredibly useful tools if bound directly to the body.

    1.  Strategy

' gives a unit several powerful benefits at the expense of inflicting the Emberdrain effect on that unit, crippling your Ember on future turns unless the unit dies. If not managed well, this can easily outweigh the benefits of this card.

If you do not have any synergies with ', it can be moderately useful as a booster spell for Morsels to make one able to soak up two extra hits and possibly hit for 12 extra damage before being eaten, negating the drawback of the Emberdrain effect.

This card is at its best when you are able to manage the Emberdrain debuff by building around it and other Emberdrain cards, either using Perils of Production or by aggressively reducing the cost of this card and other cards in the deck to Ember.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Emberstone can be a good upgrade on ', letting you play it even when you have several stacks of Emberdrain from prior turns. If playing cards on Ember is your strategy, you will also want to apply Emberstone to other spells in your deck that do not cost Ember to get them down to Ember.
|-
|
|Stackstone can be a good upgrade on ' provided you are building around it and other Emberdrain cards, doubling its already powerful effects. However, the Ember it gives is difficult to manage in this context, as unless you have Lightstone Casing this means you will not be able to get this card to Ember. This is best paired with a Perils of Production with Holdover or other strong ways to ensure you will have Perils of Production when you need it. This upgrade is very much not worth it if you aren't building around Emberdrain, as 4 Damage Shield on a unit you want dead next turn is not worth much.
|-
|
|Freezestone can be a solid upgrade on ' provided you are building around it and other Emberdrain cards, as you may not want to play it on turn 1 or 2 before you've played all your units (in the Ember spell archetype) or drawn your Holdover Perils of Production (in that archetype). Being able to play it when it's time to is worthwhile.
|-
|
|Keepstone can be a good upgrade on ' in a deck that aims to have mostly Ember spells and play Emberdrain cards at will, as playing it every turn is quite strong, and having at least one card every turn that costs Ember improves your deck's consistency. It can be good on a Holdover Perils of Production deck too, but be wary that 2 cards with Holdover will make your draw through other cards quite slow.
|}

## Votivary
' is a Melting Remnant  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore

These Reformed attendants of the Votive Houses are some of my closest allies throughout the Ring. Their reduced stature gives them a natural affinity for the weak, finding them to be kindred spirits of sorts. Their sharp resin hands, however, make their embrace of these fellow souls considerably more dangerous.

    1.  Strategy

' is one of the few cards that enables Infinites.

Votivary gives you a chump blocker who can be moved between floors whenever it dies. Thanks to its Extinguish ability, it does not slow the rate at which you cycle through your deck (nor accelerate it), unlike other Endless cards. This allows it to serve as damage mitigation while also providing Harvest triggers.

When combined with Waxer Snuffer, Votivary also provides draw acceleration, making it an incredibly useful unit as long as you have a single capacity to spare.

## Welder Helper
' is a Hellhorned  Card in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
It was a surprise to learn that even the imps craft weapons and armor for the Hellhorned. Instead of hammers, they use their innate control of fire.

    1.  Strategy

' is an excellent source of Armor for units, providing almost as much as the rare Alloy of the Ancients while being enhanced by Ashes of the Fallen and capable of being re-used with an Immortalstone.

' can be put in the front to take up to 21 damage for allies while only taking up Capacity, which is a fine use of it. It can be put in the back to provide its Armor to a different unit instead, which can be even better if you are able to kill ', as then it does not continue to take up Capacity. If you can do this reliably, Immortalstone is a great upgrade for ', as you will be able to apply its Armor repeatedly. Shardtail Queen's Imperialist upgrade line is noteworthy here for its ability to reliably kill ' and benefit from it.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
|
|Like most imps, Immortalstone can be quite strong on ', allowing you to amass a lot of armor on a single unit. This is contingent on you having a way to reliably kill ', such as Shardtail Queen's Imperialist ability, a Wickstone, or a spell that kills imps that you can reliably draw.
|-
|
| Note: Wickstone is only available if you have chosen the Melting Remnant as your primary or secondary clan - it is mentioned to help reinforce the point that having a way to reliably kill ' is strong.

Wickstone is effective on '. Most Imps are best placed on the front line so you can benefit from it's Summon effect and then it can take a hit and die, removing it from your train and freeing up the Capacity. However, you will most likely want the Armor that the ' provides to be placed on a more useful unit, and therefore the ' isn't place in the front line. This means that the ' sticks around and takes up Capacity. The Wickstone gives a way for the Welder Helper to die after it provides its Summon benefit.

|}

    1.  History
See: Version History

## Wilting Sapwood
' is a Banner Unit of the Awoken .

    1.  Lore
As an Awoken prepares to leave its physical being to become a custodian of the Wildwood, they harden their bark for ensured protection. I've observed that they also put down more roots with each step towards death, strengthening the entire Wildwood in the process.

    1.  Strategy
Wilting Sapwood is a terrible unit, especially for a Rare Banner Unit. It is an incredibly awkward tank to play, with an upfront cost of Ember and Capacity, and has poor base stats for a Capacity unit at merely Attack/Health (which is only 10 more Attack than a Husk Hermit). Unlike other Hollow units, it does not gain max Health on summon, meaning that it will usually be stuck at its mediocre starting Health without upgrades, making it very poor into Relentless combat if you attempt to use it as your main tank.

Its supposed upside - the ability to generate large amounts of Ember through its Revenge trigger - is inconsistent at best, as you usually do not know what cards you will be drawing next turn and will not always have a way to take advantage of the Ember it generates. Although Wilting Sapwood can theoretically line up big turns with a high density of X-cost spells in the deck such as Awoken's Rail Spike, ample card draw from cards like Rootseeds or Sting, and Permafrost on key cards, this is usually far too many pieces to put together in an average run, and far too much investment to justify taking an otherwise awful unit. Furthermore, the amount of times it must be hit to generate enough Ember to get those big turns into play will likely kill it outright before you can assemble those pieces.

Due to these factors, Wilting Sapwood is considered by many players to be one of the worst Banner Units in the game, if not the worst outright, and it should be avoided in almost every situation if your goal is to win the run.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades
{| class="article-table" style="width:100%"
! style="width:16%" | Upgrade
! Description
|-
| imageonly=y
| Health is not nearly enough for Wilting Sapwood to do what it needs to - it requires a Health to stick around long enough to generate any sort of value for the run with its Revenge trigger.
|-
| imageonly=y
| Endless is one way to solve the survivability issues of Wilting Sapwood - play it again after it dies, using the extra Ember generated by itself to do so. However, note that this will slow down your cycling through the deck, as you will be drawing Wilting Sapwood off the top of your deck every few turns.
|}

## Wurmkin Etchings
' is a Wurmkin  Card and a Tome in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
Even without sight or voice the Wurmkin have created a wealth of knowledge unlike anything ever seen in this Realm. Their approach to written language is ingenious, embracing knowledge and never letting it fade. By carving into the stone itself, stoneform as I’ve named it, the Wurmkin can record knowledge for nigh eternity. And even when the etchings of the stone eventually fade, the Echoes will continue to glow with the knowledge they contain.
    1.  Strategy
Keep in mind that the returned spells are random, and you cannot choose which spells get returned; despite this, Wurmkin Etchings is still one of the strongest spells in the entire game, as spells with Consume are intended to be used as powerful, one-time spells, and being able to bring 5 back at once with Wurmkin Etchings is completely game-breaking; the high Ember cost is no problem either, as the returned cards are put on top of the draw pile, not directly into the hand, so you'll have more Ember by next turn to play the returned spells.

    1.  Suggested Upgrades

## Wurmkin Spike
' is a Wurmkin  Card and a Railspike in Monster Train.

    1.  Lore
The creation of the Wurmkin Spike was an appreciated gesture, but symbolic nonetheless. The Wurmkin never opted into the Rail and had made it clear that they would continue to preserve their anonymity. When I leave — when I forget — the Spike will remain with them, their symbolic support returned along with the memory of my mistakes.

Lorewise, ' has been returned to the Wurmkin by Herzal, thus ' is not available in the Railspikes event. (Mechanically this is due to the fact that Charged Echoes are not available to unrelated Clans)

## Wyldenten
' is the Exiled Champion  of the Awoken .

Their Starter Card is Rootseeds.

    1.  Lore
Something in the Wildwood unsettles my mind, granting me visions of its very Roots when I rest there. But these Roots form a creature predating both Hollows and Channelers. Was there some soul before the Wildwood?

    1.  Battle Quotes

- For my forest!
- The millenia were not kind to us Exiles. Poor Talos lost her mind...
- Could this Pyre be a Shardsoul as well? But what could be so powerful to be trapped inside?
- What is this Rail? And this Covenant? So much has happened... So much we've missed...
- An entire forest sprung from my roots? How incredible!
- We hoped the Shardsouls would protect us...and you. It seems we only postponed the inevitable.
- Such a strange contraption we're on.
- The Exiles have failed...
- Let this serve as my redemption.
- My roots run deep!
- Do not blame Archus, he only does what the Child's Darkness compels him to.
- The souls encased within my forest don't contain the same vigor they once did...
- Be bound!
- Could this have been avoided had we ignored Herzal's plan?

    1.  Strategy
In general, Wyldenten should not be upgraded fully; crosspathing is almost always a good idea as all of his paths already gain their core effects with one level, with further levels only improving the stats, and mixing paths oftentimes provides a much stronger outcome than what would be achieved by just upgrading a single path.

    1.  Thornlord
Thornlord, thanks to their Incant trigger, works extremely well with the Stygian Guard as allies. The Stygian Guard offers other Incant units you can put on the same floor to also benefit from spells cast there, all of whom can start benefiting immediately from the extra Sting cards that Wyldenden adds to your opening hand. While not an Incant unit, Icy Cilophyte is a useful complement to Thornlord if the Strangler crosspath is not taken, able to function as both a sweeper for weak backline enemies while also applying Spell Weakness to help you take care of the larger enemies that get through.
